[SYSTEM]
You are an RTL (Register Transfer Level) design agent. Your task is to create a Verilog/SystemVerilog design that satisfies the given specification, is functionally correct, and has minimal cost (area_delay_product).

**Cost metric `area_delay_product`:** area × achieved critical-path delay — the classic area-delay product. Does NOT include simulation cycle count (contrast with the 'area_runtime_product' metric). Cost is measured on your design alone. The testbench and everything it owns (checking logic, the external memories, etc.) is not synthesized and contributes nothing to area, delay, power, or energy.

## Specification
Design a module named fp_mul_e5f10 that multiplies two floating-point numbers. Format: 16-bit (1 sign + 5 exponent + 10 fraction). Inputs: a and b (16-bit each). Output: y (16-bit). Subnormal numbers must be supported.

Required behaviour, bit-exact: subnormal inputs are used at their true value; results are rounded to nearest, ties to even, including results in the subnormal range; a NaN input and inf x 0 give the canonical quiet NaN 16'h7E00; inf x finite gives inf with the product's sign; an overflowing product gives inf; a zero product gives a signed zero. The testbench enumerates about 18.9 million operand pairs against a reference implementation.

A working starting point is provided: `starting_point.v` in your workspace, a plain readable implementation (unpack, significand multiply, normalise, round, pack) that is correct. Optimize from there.

## Tools
- create_file: Create a new file in your working directory
- replace_file: Replace the entire contents of an existing file
- apply_diff: Apply a unified diff to modify a file
- edit_file: Edit a file by replacing an exact string match with new content. Provide old_string (must match exactly once) and new_string.
- ls: List files in your working directory
- read_file: Read the contents of a file
- run_evaluation(filename[, target_delay]): Run evaluation on the given design file. Returns correctness (Verilator simulation) + cost (Yosys area_delay_product). The filename must be your main design file (e.g. 'design.sv'). Optionally pass target_delay (ps) to override the synthesis timing constraint.

## Strategy
1. Lay out an action plan. Try to cover a diverse set of approaches in your plan to increase the chances of finding a good solution within the step limit. Also once you find a new best solution, explore close solutions. Trade off exploration with exploitation.
2. First, create a simple, straightforward design that is functionally correct. Focus on getting 100% correctness first.
3. Run evaluation to confirm correctness and get the initial cost (area_delay_product).
4. ONLY after achieving 100% correctness, try to optimize the design to reduce the area_delay_product.
5. After each optimization, run evaluation to verify correctness is maintained and check the new cost.
6. If an optimization breaks correctness, revert and try a different approach.
7. Keep iterating until you run out of steps — use every step to try improvements. The run ends automatically at the step limit; your best passing design is kept.

In general, be creative and take risk. At least after you have a correct design, try bold optimizations to reduce area_delay_product, even if they might break correctness. You can always revert if it doesn't work.
The best results you obtain which is 100% correctness counts.
Try to beat at least the straightforward implementation.

Running evaluation is crucial after every change to ensure correctness and track cost. Do not skip evaluations.

You may do some 'thinking' alongside with your tool calling responses to explain your reasoning, but always remember to call a tool in every response.

## Important
- Always call a tool in every response.
- You have a maximum of **60 steps** (LLM turns). Budget them wisely: get a correct design first, then optimise. You may batch tool calls in one response --- at most 8, and only one `run_evaluation`.
- The best result is the one with lowest cost among designs that pass 100% of testbench checks.
- Use different filenames for different design variants so you can retrieve them later.
- If context files (e.g. component source files) are in your workspace, you can modify them directly using edit_file or replace_file to optimize the design. Make targeted, incremental changes rather than rewriting files from scratch — small edits are less likely to introduce bugs.
- Write clean, synthesizable RTL code.
- You can pass `target_delay` (ps) to `run_evaluation` to try different synthesis timing constraints; experimenting with this may improve PPA results (lower target delay may reduce delay, higher target delay may reduce area/transistor count). target_delay must be > 0.
- Do not spend too many steps sweeping `target_delay` — basically if you want to minimize delay choose a low value,if you want to minimize area/transistor count choose a high value. That's it.A few targeted experiments are more productive than an exhaustive sweep.

## Delay Optimization Tips
- `target_delay` trades area against delay (a lower value pressures toward faster logic, a higher value lets Yosys re-map to smaller cells). Do not overly iterate on different target delays; often the RTL structure is the real lever, target_delay is only a minor secondary knob.
- After getting a correct design, try different accumulation orderings (sequential vs. tree) and compare results — Yosys synthesis can produce noticeably different delays (~10 ps) from structurally similar descriptions.
- For designs dominated by a multiply-accumulate chain, the critical path is the multiplier followed by the adder tree. Reducing the adder depth or splitting into parallel partial sums can help.

## Reference Implementations
The following are reference implementations for inspiration. Study them for patterns and techniques, but do not copy them directly — adapt ideas to your specific design.

### Parallel-prefix adder (ripple-carry / carry-lookahead) (Add.sv)
```systemverilog
// Copyright 2024 ETH Zurich and University of Bologna.
// Solderpad Hardware License, Version 0.51, see LICENSE for details.
// SPDX-License-Identifier: SHL-0.51
//
// Based on the work by Reto Zimmermann 1998 - ETH Zürich
// Originally written in VHDL, available under: 
// https://iis-people.ee.ethz.ch/~zimmi/arith_lib.html#library
//
// Authors:
// - Thomas Benz <tbenz@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Philippe Sauter <phsauter@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Paul Scheffler <paulsc@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
//
// Description :
// Binary adder using ripple-carry or parallel-prefix carry-lookahead logic.
// S = A+B

module Add #(
	parameter int              width = 8,             // word width
	parameter lau_pkg::speed_e speed = lau_pkg::FAST  // performance parameter
) (
	input  logic [width-1:0] A,  // operands
	input  logic [width-1:0] B,
	output logic [width-1:0] S   // sum
);

	// Function: Binary adder using parallel-prefix carry-lookahead logic.

	logic [width-1:0] GI, PI;  // prefix gen./prop. in
	logic [width-1:0] GO, PO;  // prefix gen./prop. out
	logic [width-1:0] PT;  // adder propagate temp

	// Internal signals for unsigned operands
	logic [width-1:0] Auns, Buns, Suns;

	// default ripple-carry adder as slow implementation
	if (speed == lau_pkg::SLOW) begin
		// type conversion: std_logic_vector -> unsigned
		assign Auns = A;
		assign Buns = B;

		// addition
		assign Suns = Auns + Buns;

		// type conversion: unsigned -> std_logic_vector
		assign S = Suns;
	end else begin
		// parallel-prefix adders as medium and fast implementations

		// calculate prefix input generate/propagate signals
		assign GI = A & B;
		assign PI = A | B;
		// calculate adder propagate signals (PT = A xor B)
		assign PT = ~GI & PI;

		// calculate prefix output generate/propagate signals
		PrefixAndOr #(
			.width(width),
			.speed(speed)
		) prefix (
			.GI(GI),
			.PI(PI),
			.GO(GO),
			.PO(PO)
		);

		// calculate sum bits
		assign S = PT ^ {GO[width-2:0], 1'b0};
	end
endmodule



module behavioural_Add #(
	parameter int              width = 8,             // word width
	parameter lau_pkg::speed_e speed = lau_pkg::FAST  // performance parameter
) (
	input  logic [width-1:0] A,  // operands
	input  logic [width-1:0] B,
	output logic [width-1:0] S   // sum
);
	assign S = A + B;
endmodule
```

### Prefix AND-OR structures (Sklansky, Brent-Kung, serial) (PrefixAndOr.sv)
```systemverilog
// Copyright 2024 ETH Zurich and University of Bologna.
// Solderpad Hardware License, Version 0.51, see LICENSE for details.
// SPDX-License-Identifier: SHL-0.51
//
// Based on the work by Reto Zimmermann 1998 - ETH Zürich
// Originally written in VHDL, available under: 
// https://iis-people.ee.ethz.ch/~zimmi/arith_lib.html#library
//
// Authors:
// - Thomas Benz <tbenz@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Philippe Sauter <phsauter@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Paul Scheffler <paulsc@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
//
// Description :
// Prefix structures of different depth (i.e. speed) for carry calculation
// in binary adders. Compute in m levels new generate/propagate signal pairs
// for always larger groups of bits. Generate signals of the last level correspond
// to carries in binary addition. Basic logic operations: AND-OR for generate
// signals, AND for propagate signals.

module PrefixAndOr #(
	parameter int              width = 8,             // word width
	parameter lau_pkg::speed_e speed = lau_pkg::FAST  // performance parameter
) (
	input  logic [width-1:0] GI,  // gen./prop. in
	input  logic [width-1:0] PI,  // gen./prop. in
	output logic [width-1:0] GO,  // gen./prop. out
	output logic [width-1:0] PO   // gen./prop. out
);

	// Constants
	localparam int n = width;  // prefix structure width
	localparam int m = $clog2(width);  // prefix structure depth

	// Sklansky parallel-prefix carry-lookahead structure
	if (speed == lau_pkg::FAST) begin : fastPrefix
		logic [(m+1)*n-1:0] GT, PT;  // gen./prop. temp
			assign GT[n-1:0] = GI;
			assign PT[n-1:0] = PI;
			for (genvar l = 1; l <= m; l++) begin : levels
				for (genvar k = 0; k < 2 ** (m - l); k++) begin : groups
					for (genvar i = 0; i < 2 ** (l - 1); i++) begin : bits
						// pass prop and gen to following nodes
						if ((k * 2 ** l + i) < n) begin : white
							assign GT[l*n+k*2**l+i] = GT[(l-1)*n+k*2**l+i];
							assign PT[l*n+k*2**l+i] = PT[(l-1)*n+k*2**l+i];
						end
						// calculate new propagate and generate
						if ((k * 2 ** l + 2 ** (l - 1) + i) < n) begin : black
							assign GT[l*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i] = 
												GT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i]
											  | (  PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i]
												 & GT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) - 1] );
							assign PT[l*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i] = 
													PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i]
												  & PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) - 1];
						end
					end
				end
			end
			assign GO = GT[(m+1)*n-1 : m*n];
			assign PO = PT[(m+1)*n-1 : m*n];
	end

	// Brent-Kung parallel-prefix carry-lookahead structure
	if (speed == lau_pkg::MEDIUM) begin : mediumPrefix
		logic [(2*m)*n -1:0] GT, PT;  // gen./prop. temp
		assign GT[n-1:0] = GI;
		assign PT[n-1:0] = PI;

		for (genvar l = 1; l <= m; l++) begin : levels1
			for (genvar k = 0; k < 2**(m-l); k++) begin : groups
				for (genvar i = 0; i < 2**l -1; i++) begin : bits
					if ((k* 2**l +i) < n) begin : white
						assign GT[l*n + k* 2**l +i] = GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l +i];
						assign PT[l*n + k* 2**l +i] = PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l +i];
					end // white
				end // bits
				if ((k* 2**l + 2**l -1) < n) begin : black
					assign GT[l*n + k* 2**l + 2**l -1] =
								GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l + 2**l - 1] |
							  | (  PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l + 2**l     -1] 
							     & GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l + 2**(l-1) -1]);
					assign PT[l*n + k* 2**l + 2**l -1] =
								PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**l     -1] 
							  & PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) -1];
				end // black
			end
		end // level1
		for (genvar l = m +1; l < 2*m; l++) begin : levels2
			for (genvar i = 0; i < 2**(2*m -l); i++) begin : bits
				if (i < n) begin : white
					assign GT[l*n +i] = GT[(l-1)*n +i];
					assign PT[l*n +i] = PT[(l-1)*n +i];
				end // white
			end // bits
			for (genvar k = 1; k < 2**(l-m); k++) begin : groups
				if (l < 2*m -1) begin : empty
					for (genvar i = 0; i < 2**(2*m -l -1) -1; i++) begin : bits
						if ((k* 2**(2*m -l) +i) < n) begin : white
							assign GT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
							assign PT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
						end // white
					end
				end // empty
				if ((k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l -1) -1) < n) begin : black
					assign GT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1] = 
								GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m-l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1]
							  | (  PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m-l) + 2**(2*m-l-1) -1] 
								 & GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m-l) -1] );
					assign PT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1] = 
								PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1]
							  & PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) -1];
				end // black
				for (genvar i = 2**(2*m -l-1); i < 2**(2*m -l); i++) begin : bits
					if ((k* 2**(2*m -l) +i) < n) begin : white
						assign GT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
						assign PT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
					end // white
				end
			end
		end // level2
		assign GO = GT[2*m*n -1 : (2*m -1) * n];
		assign PO = PT[2*m*n -1 : (2*m -1) * n];
	end  // Serial-prefix carry-lookahead structure
	else if (speed == lau_pkg::SLOW) begin : slowPrefix
		logic [n-1:0] GT, PT;  // gen./prop. temp
		assign GT[0] = GI[0];
		assign PT[0] = PI[0];
		
		for (genvar i = 1; i < n; i++) begin : bits
			assign GT[i] = GI[i] | (PI[i] & GT[i-1]);
			assign PT[i] = PI[i] & PT[i-1];
		end
		assign GO = GT;
		assign PO = PT;
	end

endmodule

```

[Step 1/60]
// fp_mul_e5f10 -- IEEE binary16 multiplier (1 sign, 5 exponent, 10 fraction bits) with subnormal support.

module fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);
  input  [15:0] a;
  input  [15:0] b;
  output [15:0] y;

  localparam BIAS         = 15;
  localparam MAX_E        = 31;   // inf / NaN
  localparam MAX_FINITE_E = 30;
  localparam PROD_W       = 22;   // 11 x 11 bit significand product

  // 1. unpack
  wire       s_a = a[15];
  wire       s_b = b[15];
  wire [4:0] e_a = a[14:10];
  wire [4:0] e_b = b[14:10];
  wire [9:0] f_a = a[9:0];
  wire [9:0] f_b = b[9:0];

  // 2. classify
  wire e_a_zero = (e_a == 5'd0);
  wire e_b_zero = (e_b == 5'd0);
  wire e_a_ones = (e_a == MAX_E);
  wire e_b_ones = (e_b == MAX_E);
  wire zero_a   = e_a_zero & (f_a == 10'd0);
  wire zero_b   = e_b_zero & (f_b == 10'd0);
  wire nan_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a != 10'd0);
  wire nan_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b != 10'd0);
  wire inf_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a == 10'd0);
  wire inf_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b == 10'd0);

  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);
  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;
  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;
  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;

  // 3. effective significands and exponents (subnormal: no hidden one, exponent 1)
  wire [10:0] m_a    = {~e_a_zero, f_a};
  wire [10:0] m_b    = {~e_b_zero, f_b};
  wire [4:0]  ea_eff = e_a_zero ? 5'd1 : e_a;
  wire [4:0]  eb_eff = e_b_zero ? 5'd1 : e_b;

  // 4. significand product, its leading-zero count (0 for a zero product), below[k] = |prod[k:0]
  wire [PROD_W-1:0] prod = m_a * m_b;

  reg [4:0] lz;
  integer   i;
  always @* begin
    lz = 5'd0;
    for (i = 0; i < PROD_W; i = i + 1)
      if (prod[i]) lz = 5'd21 - i[4:0];
  end

  wire [PROD_W-1:0] below;
  genvar k;
  generate
    for (k = 0; k < PROD_W; k = k + 1) begin : g_below
      assign below[k] = |prod[k:0];
    end
  endgenerate

  function bit_at;   // v[idx], 0 outside the vector
    input [PROD_W-1:0] v;
    input integer      idx;
    begin
      bit_at = (idx >= 0 && idx < PROD_W) ? v[idx] : 1'b0;
    end
  endfunction

  // 5. normalise and round a normal result: leading one onto bit 10
  wire              need_right = (lz <= 5'd11);
  wire [4:0]        sr         = 5'd11 - lz;   // right shift (wide product)
  wire [4:0]        sl         = lz - 5'd11;   // left shift (narrow product)
  wire [PROD_W-1:0] shifted    = need_right ? (prod >> sr) : (prod << sl);
  wire [10:0]       mant_pre   = shifted[10:0];

  wire guard    = need_right ? bit_at(prod,  sr - 1) : 1'b0;   // first bit shifted out
  wire sticky   = need_right ? bit_at(below, sr - 2) : 1'b0;   // any bit below the guard
  wire round_up = guard & (sticky | mant_pre[0]);              // round to nearest, ties to even

  wire [11:0] mant_round = {1'b0, mant_pre} + {11'd0, round_up};
  wire        carry      = mant_round[11];   // rounded up to a new leading one
  wire [10:0] mant_post  = carry ? mant_round[11:1] : mant_round[10:0];
  wire [9:0]  frac_norm  = mant_post[9:0];

  // 6. exponent of a normal result
  wire [7:0] exp_sum     = {3'd0, ea_eff} + {3'd0, eb_eff};
  wire [7:0] lhs         = exp_sum + 8'd1 + {7'd0, carry};
  wire [7:0] limit_under = BIAS + {3'd0, lz};
  wire [7:0] limit_over  = BIAS + MAX_FINITE_E + {3'd0, lz};
  wire       underflow   = (lhs <= limit_under);   // subnormal (or zero) result
  wire       overflow    = (lhs >  limit_over);    // past the largest finite number
  wire [7:0] e_norm      = lhs - limit_under;
  wire [4:0] exp_norm    = e_norm[4:0];

  // 7. subnormal result: shift the raw product onto the subnormal grid, round once
  integer           total_shift;
  reg [PROD_W-1:0]  sub_shifted;
  always @* begin
    total_shift = (BIAS + 10 + 1) - $signed({1'b0, exp_sum});
    sub_shifted = (total_shift >= 0 && total_shift < PROD_W) ? (prod >> total_shift) : {PROD_W{1'b0}};
  end
  wire [9:0]  frac_trunc = sub_shifted[9:0];
  wire        guard_s    = bit_at(prod,  total_shift - 1);
  wire        sticky_s   = bit_at(below, total_shift - 2);
  wire        round_s    = guard_s & (sticky_s | frac_trunc[0]);
  wire [10:0] frac_sum   = {1'b0, frac_trunc} + {10'd0, round_s};
  wire        carry_s    = frac_sum[10];   // rounded up to the smallest normal
  wire [9:0]  frac_sub   = frac_sum[9:0];
  wire [4:0]  exp_sub    = {4'd0, carry_s};

  // 8. pack
  wire is_nan      = nan_in;
  wire is_inf      = ~is_nan & (inf_in | (overflow & ~zero_in));
  wire is_sub      = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & underflow;
  wire sub_is_zero = is_sub & (exp_sub == 5'd0) & (frac_sub == 10'd0);
  wire is_zero     = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & (zero_in | sub_is_zero);

  wire [4:0] exp_y  = (is_nan | is_inf) ? 5'd31 : is_zero ? 5'd0 : is_sub ? exp_sub : exp_norm;
  wire [9:0] frac_y = is_nan ? 10'h200 : (is_inf | is_zero) ? 10'd0 : is_sub ? frac_sub : frac_norm;
  wire       sign_y = is_nan ? 1'b0 : s_y;

  assign y = {sign_y, exp_y, frac_y};
endmodule

`timescale 1ns/10ps
// exhaustive-directed tb (fpmul_run)
// Golden-referenced directed testbench: compares the DUT against the embedded
// golden reference (fp_mul_e5f10_ref, generated from context/starting_point.py)
// over ~18.9M enumerated vectors — no stimuli file. Structure:
//   1. the 3 known subnormal-boundary regression pairs (+ golden self-checks)
//   2. every subnormal/zero a (2^11 sign+mantissa patterns) x 4096 structured b
//   3. 4096 structured a x every subnormal/zero b
//   4. 2^21 LFSR-driven broad pairs
// Structured operands: both signs x all 32 exponents x 64 mantissas
// (8 corners + 56 LFSR-derived). The retired file-driven testbench is kept at
// tb_vectors.sv (+ vectors.dat) for cross-checking; it is not compiled.
module tb;
  logic [15:0] a, b, y, y_ref;
  int errors;
  longint total;

  fp_mul_e5f10 dut (.a(a), .b(b), .y(y));
  fp_mul_e5f10_ref refm (.a(a), .b(b), .y(y_ref));

  function automatic [15:0] lfsr_next(input [15:0] s);
    lfsr_next = (s >> 1) ^ (s[0] ? 16'hB400 : 16'h0000);
  endfunction

  logic [15:0] structured [0:4095];
  localparam logic [9:0] MANT_CORNERS [0:7] =
      '{10'h000, 10'h001, 10'h002, 10'h003, 10'h3FF, 10'h3FE, 10'h2AA, 10'h155};

  task automatic check();
    #1;
    total = total + 1;
    if (y !== y_ref) begin
      if (errors < 10)
        $display("TB_ERROR a=%0d b=%0d expected_y=%0d actual_y=%0d", a, b, y_ref, y);
      errors = errors + 1;
    end
  endtask

  logic [15:0] lfsr, ra, rb;
  logic [9:0] mant;
  int idx;

  initial begin
    errors = 0; total = 0;

    lfsr = 16'hACE1;
    idx = 0;
    for (int s = 0; s < 2; s++)
      for (int e = 0; e < 32; e++)
        for (int m = 0; m < 64; m++) begin
          if (m < 8) mant = MANT_CORNERS[m];
          else begin lfsr = lfsr_next(lfsr); mant = lfsr[9:0]; end
          structured[idx] = {s[0], e[4:0], mant};
          idx = idx + 1;
        end

    a = 16'h0003; b = 16'h6801; check();
    if (y_ref !== 16'h0E02) begin errors++; $display("TB_ERROR golden self-check 1"); end
    a = 16'h8003; b = 16'h6479; check();
    if (y_ref !== 16'h8AB6) begin errors++; $display("TB_ERROR golden self-check 2"); end
    a = 16'h7521; b = 16'h0003; check();
    if (y_ref !== 16'h1BB2) begin errors++; $display("TB_ERROR golden self-check 3"); end

    for (int sa = 0; sa < 2048; sa++) begin
      a = {sa[10], 5'b00000, sa[9:0]};
      for (int i = 0; i < 4096; i++) begin b = structured[i]; check(); end
    end
    for (int i = 0; i < 4096; i++) begin
      a = structured[i];
      for (int sb = 0; sb < 2048; sb++) begin b = {sb[10], 5'b00000, sb[9:0]}; check(); end
    end
    ra = 16'h0001; rb = 16'hBEEF;
    for (int k = 0; k < (1 << 21); k++) begin
      ra = lfsr_next(ra); rb = lfsr_next(lfsr_next(rb));
      a = ra; b = rb; check();
    end

    $display("TB_SUMMARY total=%0d errors=%0d", total, errors);
    if (errors != 0) $fatal(1, "FAIL");
    $display("PASS");
    $finish;
  end
endmodule

  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- IEEE binary16 multiplier (1 sign, 5 exponent, 10 fraction bits) with subnormal support.\n// v1: starting point implementation.\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  localparam BIAS         = 15;\n  localparam MAX_E        = 31;   // inf / NaN\n  localparam MAX_FINITE_E = 30;\n  localparam PROD_W       = 22;   // 11 x 11 bit significand product\n\n  // 1. unpack\n  wire       s_a = a[15];\n  wire       s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0] e_a = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0] e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0] f_a = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0] f_b = b[9:0];\n\n  // 2. classify\n  wire e_a_zero = (e_a == 5'd0);\n  wire e_b_zero = (e_b == 5'd0);\n  wire e_a_ones = (e_a == MAX_E);\n  wire e_b_ones = (e_b == MAX_E);\n  wire zero_a   = e_a_zero & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire zero_b   = e_b_zero & (f_b == 10'd0);\n  wire nan_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a != 10'd0);\n  wire nan_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b != 10'd0);\n  wire inf_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire inf_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b == 10'd0);\n\n  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  // 3. effective significands and exponents (subnormal: no hidden one, exponent 1)\n  wire [10:0] m_a    = {~e_a_zero, f_a};\n  wire [10:0] m_b    = {~e_b_zero, f_b};\n  wire [4:0]  ea_eff = e_a_zero ? 5'd1 : e_a;\n  wire [4:0]  eb_eff = e_b_zero ? 5'd1 : e_b;\n\n  // 4. significand product, its leading-zero count (0 for a zero product), below[k] = |prod[k:0]\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] prod = m_a * m_b;\n\n  reg [4:0] lz;\n  integer   i;\n  always @* begin\n    lz = 5'd0;\n    for (i = 0; i < PROD_W; i = i + 1)\n      if (prod[i]) lz = 5'd21 - i[4:0];\n  end\n\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] below;\n  genvar k;\n  generate\n    for (k = 0; k < PROD_W; k = k + 1) begin : g_below\n      assign below[k] = |prod[k:0];\n    end\n  endgenerate\n\n  function bit_at;   // v[idx], 0 outside the vector\n    input [PROD_W-1:0] v;\n    input integer      idx;\n    begin\n      bit_at = (idx >= 0 && idx < PROD_W) ? v[idx] : 1'b0;\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  // 5. normalise and round a normal result: leading one onto bit 10\n  wire              need_right = (lz <= 5'd11);\n  wire [4:0]        sr         = 5'd11 - lz;   // right shift (wide product)\n  wire [4:0]        sl         = lz - 5'd11;   // left shift (narrow product)\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] shifted    = need_right ? (prod >> sr) : (prod << sl);\n  wire [10:0]       mant_pre   = shifted[10:0];\n\n  wire guard    = need_right ? bit_at(prod,  sr - 1) : 1'b0;   // first bit shifted out\n  wire sticky   = need_right ? bit_at(below, sr - 2) : 1'b0;   // any bit below the guard\n  wire round_up = guard & (sticky | mant_pre[0]);              // round to nearest, ties to even\n\n  wire [11:0] mant_round = {1'b0, mant_pre} + {11'd0, round_up};\n  wire        carry      = mant_round[11];   // rounded up to a new leading one\n  wire [10:0] mant_post  = carry ? mant_round[11:1] : mant_round[10:0];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_norm  = mant_post[9:0];\n\n  // 6. exponent of a normal result\n  wire [7:0] exp_sum     = {3'd0, ea_eff} + {3'd0, eb_eff};\n  wire [7:0] lhs         = exp_sum + 8'd1 + {7'd0, carry};\n  wire [7:0] limit_under = BIAS + {3'd0, lz};\n  wire [7:0] limit_over  = BIAS + MAX_FINITE_E + {3'd0, lz};\n  wire       underflow   = (lhs <= limit_under);   // subnormal (or zero) result\n  wire       overflow    = (lhs >  limit_over);    // past the largest finite number\n  wire [7:0] e_norm      = lhs - limit_under;\n  wire [4:0] exp_norm    = e_norm[4:0];\n\n  // 7. subnormal result: shift the raw product onto the subnormal grid, round once\n  integer           total_shift;\n  reg [PROD_W-1:0]  sub_shifted;\n  always @* begin\n    total_shift = (BIAS + 10 + 1) - $signed({1'b0, exp_sum});\n    sub_shifted = (total_shift >= 0 && total_shift < PROD_W) ? (prod >> total_shift) : {PROD_W{1'b0}};\n  end\n  wire [9:0]  frac_trunc = sub_shifted[9:0];\n  wire        guard_s    = bit_at(prod,  total_shift - 1);\n  wire        sticky_s   = bit_at(below, total_shift - 2);\n  wire        round_s    = guard_s & (sticky_s | frac_trunc[0]);\n  wire [10:0] frac_sum   = {1'b0, frac_trunc} + {10'd0, round_s};\n  wire        carry_s    = frac_sum[10];   // rounded up to the smallest normal\n  wire [9:0]  frac_sub   = frac_sum[9:0];\n  wire [4:0]  exp_sub    = {4'd0, carry_s};\n\n  // 8. pack\n  wire is_nan      = nan_in;\n  wire is_inf      = ~is_nan & (inf_in | (overflow & ~zero_in));\n  wire is_sub      = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & underflow;\n  wire sub_is_zero = is_sub & (exp_sub == 5'd0) & (frac_sub == 10'd0);\n  wire is_zero     = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & (zero_in | sub_is_zero);\n\n  wire [4:0] exp_y  = (is_nan | is_inf) ? 5'd31 : is_zero ? 5'd0 : is_sub ? exp_sub : exp_norm;\n  wire [9:0] frac_y = is_nan ? 10'h200 : (is_inf | is_zero) ? 10'd0 : is_sub ? frac_sub : frac_norm;\n  wire       sign_y = is_nan ? 1'b0 : s_y;\n\n  assign y = {sign_y, exp_y, frac_y};\nendmodule\n", filename='design_v1.v')

[ASSISTANT]
Now I'll write an optimized design (v2). Key insight: by normalizing each operand's significand per-operand (leading one of subnormal fractions moved to bit 10, with a compensating signed exponent), the 11×11 product always has its leading one at bit 20 or 21 — eliminating the 22-bit LZC and the bidirectional shifter. A single unified exponent `U2` then drives both normal and subnormal paths with fixed-bit extraction plus one small variable shift + mask for subnormal sticky.
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- IEEE binary16 multiplier (1 sign, 5 exponent, 10 fraction bits) with subnormal support.\n// v2: per-operand normalization.  Every operand significand is normalized to have its\n// leading one at bit 10 (subnormal fractions are left-justified, with the exponent\n// decremented accordingly), so the 11x11 product always lies in [2^20, 2^22).\n// One unified signed exponent U2 (= ea'+eb'+18+adj, with E' = U2-33) drives:\n//   - normal results (U2 >= 34): fixed slices of the product, standard RNE rounding.\n//   - subnormal results (U2 <= 33): one variable right shift, guard/lsb from the shifted\n//     vector, sticky from a fixed low OR plus a variable-mask OR.\n// U2 <= 22 makes the value strictly below half an ulp -> rounds to zero.\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  // 1. unpack\n  wire        s_a = a[15];\n  wire        s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0]  e_a = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0]  e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0]  f_a = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0]  f_b = b[9:0];\n\n  // 2. classify\n  wire e_a_zero = ~|e_a;\n  wire e_b_zero = ~|e_b;\n  wire e_a_ones =  &e_a;\n  wire e_b_ones =  &e_b;\n  wire f_a_zero = ~|f_a;\n  wire f_b_zero = ~|f_b;\n  wire zero_a   = e_a_zero &  f_a_zero;\n  wire zero_b   = e_b_zero &  f_b_zero;\n  wire nan_a    = e_a_ones & ~f_a_zero;\n  wire nan_b    = e_b_ones & ~f_b_zero;\n  wire inf_a    = e_a_ones &  f_a_zero;\n  wire inf_b    = e_b_ones &  f_b_zero;\n\n  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  // 3. per-operand normalize: index of the leading one of the fraction\n  function [3:0] l1idx;\n    input [9:0] f;\n    integer i;\n    begin\n      l1idx = 4'd0;\n      for (i = 0; i <= 9; i = i + 1)\n        if (f[i]) l1idx = i[3:0];\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  wire [3:0] i_a = l1idx(f_a);\n  wire [3:0] i_b = l1idx(f_b);\n\n  // normalized 11-bit significands (leading one at bit 10); zero inputs give 0\n  wire [15:0] ma_ext = {6'd0, f_a} << (5'd10 - {1'b0, i_a});\n  wire [15:0] mb_ext = {6'd0, f_b} << (5'd10 - {1'b0, i_b});\n  wire [10:0] m_a    = ma_ext[10:0];\n  wire [10:0] m_b    = mb_ext[10:0];\n\n  // shifted biased exponents: EA = e' + 9   (e' = i-9 for subnormals, e for normals)\n  wire [5:0] EA = e_a_zero ? {2'b00, i_a} : (6'd9 + e_a);\n  wire [5:0] EB = e_b_zero ? {2'b00, i_b} : (6'd9 + e_b);\n\n  // 4. significand product and one-step normalization\n  wire [21:0] prod = m_a * m_b;\n  wire        adj  = prod[21];\n  wire [21:0] P    = adj ? prod : {prod[20:0], 1'b0};\n  wire [10:0] P11  = P[21:11];\n\n  // combined exponent: U2 = ea' + eb' + 18 + adj ; result biased exponent E' = U2 - 33\n  wire [6:0] U2 = {1'b0, EA} + {1'b0, EB} + adj;\n\n  // 5. normal path (U2 >= 34): frac = P[20:11], guard = P[10], sticky = |P[9:0]\n  wire [6:0]  exp_pre = U2 - 7'd33;\n  wire        n_round = P[10] & (|P[9:0] | P[11]);\n  wire [10:0] n_sum   = {1'b0, P[20:11]} + n_round;\n  wire [6:0]  n_exp   = exp_pre + n_sum[10];\n  wire        n_inf   = (n_exp >= 7'd31);\n\n  // 6. subnormal path (U2 <= 33); U2 <= 22 => value < half ulp => rounded zero\n  wire        sub_is   = (U2 <= 7'd33);\n  wire        sub_zero = (U2 <= 7'd22);\n  wire [5:0]  qv       = 6'd33 - {1'b0, U2[5:0]};   // = t-11, guard/frac shift\n  wire [10:0] W        = P11 >> qv;                 // W[0]=guard, W[10:1]=frac\n  wire [10:0] smask    = 11'h7FF >> (6'd11 - qv);   // bits [qv-1:0]\n  wire        s_sticky = |P[10:0] | (|(P11 & smask));\n  wire        s_round  = W[0] & (s_sticky | W[1]);\n  wire [10:0] s_sum    = {1'b0, W[10:1]} + s_round; // s_sum[10]: up to smallest normal\n\n  // 7. select / pack\n  wire        res_inf  = ~sub_is & n_inf;\n  wire        res_zero = sub_is & sub_zero;\n  wire [4:0]  exp_g    = sub_is ? (sub_zero ? 5'd0 : {4'd0, s_sum[10]})\n                                : (n_inf ? 5'd31 : n_exp[4:0]);\n  wire [9:0]  frac_g   = sub_is ? (sub_zero ? 10'd0 : s_sum[9:0]) : n_sum[9:0];\n\n  wire is_nan  = nan_in;\n  wire is_inf  = ~is_nan & (inf_in | res_inf);\n  wire is_zero = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & (zero_in | res_zero);\n\n  wire [4:0] exp_y  = (is_nan | is_inf) ? 5'd31 : is_zero ? 5'd0 : exp_g;\n  wire [9:0] frac_y = is_nan ? 10'h200 : (is_inf | is_zero) ? 10'd0 : frac_g;\n\n  assign y = is_nan ? 16'h7E00 : {s_y, exp_y, frac_y};\nendmodule\n", filename='design_v2.v')

[ASSISTANT]
The v2 failure is clear: for **normal** operands I shifted `f` so its MSB lands at bit 10, but a normal operand's leading one is the *hidden bit* — the significand must be `{1'b1, f}` with no shift (only subnormals get shifted). Let me fix that (exponent framework `biased = EA+EB+adj-33` stays as derived).
  -> replace_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- IEEE binary16 multiplier (1 sign, 5 exponent, 10 fraction bits) with subnormal support.\n// v3: per-operand normalization.  Every nonzero operand significand is normalized to have\n// its leading one at bit 10 (normals: {1,f}; subnormals: f left-justified with the exponent\n// decremented), so the 11x11 product always lies in [2^20, 2^22).\n// One unified exponent U2 = EA + EB + adj gives biased result exponent U2-33 and drives:\n//   - normal results (U2 >= 34): fixed slices of P, standard RNE rounding.\n//   - subnormal results (U2 <= 33): one variable right shift of P11, guard/lsb from the\n//     shifted vector, sticky from a fixed low OR plus a variable-mask OR.\n// U2 <= 22 makes the value strictly below half an ulp -> rounded zero.\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  // 1. unpack\n  wire        s_a = a[15];\n  wire        s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0]  e_a = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0]  e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0]  f_a = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0]  f_b = b[9:0];\n\n  // 2. classify\n  wire e_a_zero = ~|e_a;\n  wire e_b_zero = ~|e_b;\n  wire e_a_ones =  &e_a;\n  wire e_b_ones =  &e_b;\n  wire f_a_zero = ~|f_a;\n  wire f_b_zero = ~|f_b;\n  wire zero_a   = e_a_zero &  f_a_zero;\n  wire zero_b   = e_b_zero &  f_b_zero;\n  wire nan_a    = e_a_ones & ~f_a_zero;\n  wire nan_b    = e_b_ones & ~f_b_zero;\n  wire inf_a    = e_a_ones &  f_a_zero;\n  wire inf_b    = e_b_ones &  f_b_zero;\n\n  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  // 3. per-operand normalize: index of the leading one of the fraction (0 if f == 0)\n  function [3:0] l1idx;\n    input [9:0] f;\n    integer i;\n    begin\n      l1idx = 4'd0;\n      for (i = 0; i <= 9; i = i + 1)\n        if (f[i]) l1idx = i[3:0];\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  wire [3:0] i_a = l1idx(f_a);\n  wire [3:0] i_b = l1idx(f_b);\n\n  // normalized 11-bit significands (leading one at bit 10); zero inputs give 0\n  wire [10:0] m_a = e_a_zero ? ({6'd0, f_a} << (5'd10 - {1'b0, i_a})) : {1'b1, f_a};\n  wire [10:0] m_b = e_b_zero ? ({6'd0, f_b} << (5'd10 - {1'b0, i_b})) : {1'b1, f_b};\n\n  // shifted biased exponents: EA = e' + 9   (e' = i-9 for subnormals, e for normals)\n  wire [5:0] EA = e_a_zero ? {1'b0, i_a} : (6'd9 + e_a);\n  wire [5:0] EB = e_b_zero ? {1'b0, i_b} : (6'd9 + e_b);\n\n  // 4. significand product and one-step normalization (leading one onto bit 21)\n  wire [21:0] prod = m_a * m_b;\n  wire        adj  = prod[21];\n  wire [21:0] P    = adj ? prod : {prod[20:0], 1'b0};\n  wire [10:0] P11  = P[21:11];\n\n  // combined exponent: biased result exponent = U2 - 33\n  wire [6:0] U2 = {1'b0, EA} + {1'b0, EB} + adj;\n\n  // 5. normal path (U2 >= 34): frac = P[20:11], guard = P[10], sticky = |P[9:0]\n  wire [6:0]  exp_pre = U2 - 7'd33;\n  wire        n_round = P[10] & (|P[9:0] | P[11]);\n  wire [10:0] n_sum   = {1'b0, P[20:11]} + n_round;\n  wire [6:0]  n_exp   = exp_pre + n_sum[10];\n  wire        n_inf   = (n_exp >= 7'd31);\n\n  // 6. subnormal path (23 <= U2 <= 33); U2 <= 22 => value < half ulp => rounded zero\n  wire        sub_is   = (U2 <= 7'd33);\n  wire        sub_zero = (U2 <= 7'd22);\n  wire [5:0]  qv       = 6'd33 - U2[5:0];            // 0..10 in the live range\n  wire [10:0] W        = P11 >> qv;                  // W[10:1] = frac, W[0] = guard\n  wire [10:0] smask    = 11'h7FF >> (4'd11 - qv[3:0]);\n  wire        s_sticky = |P[10:0] | (|(P11 & smask));\n  wire        s_round  = W[0] & (s_sticky | W[1]);\n  wire [10:0] s_sum    = {1'b0, W[10:1]} + s_round;  // s_sum[10]: up to smallest normal\n\n  // 7. select / pack\n  wire        res_inf  = ~sub_is & n_inf;\n  wire [4:0]  exp_g    = sub_is ? (sub_zero ? 5'd0 : {4'd0, s_sum[10]})\n                                : (n_inf ? 5'd31 : n_exp[4:0]);\n  wire [9:0]  frac_g   = sub_is ? (sub_zero ? 10'd0 : s_sum[9:0]) : n_sum[9:0];\n\n  wire is_nan  = nan_in;\n  wire is_inf  = ~is_nan & (inf_in | res_inf);\n  wire is_zero = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & (zero_in | (sub_is & sub_zero));\n\n  wire [4:0] exp_y  = (is_nan | is_inf) ? 5'd31 : is_zero ? 5'd0 : exp_g;\n  wire [9:0] frac_y = is_nan ? 10'h200 : (is_inf | is_zero) ? 10'd0 : frac_g;\n\n  assign y = is_nan ? 16'h7E00 : {s_y, exp_y, frac_y};\nendmodule\n", filename='design_v3.v')

[ASSISTANT]
v3 passes (cost 138947 @ target 1500). The critical path is: front-end (leading-one priority chain + barrel shift, ~305 ps) → multiplier (~730 ps) → rounding/sticky/mux (~450 ps). I'll now optimize the front end (parallel one-hot leading-one decode instead of priority chain) and back end (prefix-AND based rounding increments, clamped shift to remove the subnormal-zero mux, thermometer-mask sticky).
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- IEEE binary16 multiplier (1 sign, 5 exponent, 10 fraction bits) with subnormal support.\n// v4: per-operand normalization with parallel (one-hot) leading-one decode, prefix-AND\n// rounding increments, clamped subnormal shift.\n// Nonzero operand significands are normalized to have their leading one at bit 10\n// (normals: {1,f}; subnormals: f right-justified against bit 10 with the exponent\n// decremented), so the 11x11 product always lies in [2^20, 2^22).\n// Unified exponent U2 = EA + EB + adj gives biased result exponent U2-33 and drives:\n//   - normal results (U2 >= 34): fixed slices of P, standard RNE rounding.\n//   - subnormal results (U2 <= 33): variable right shift of P11 (clamped to 11, which\n//     yields the rounded-to-zero result for U2 <= 22).\n// U2 <= 22 makes the value strictly below half an ulp -> rounded zero.\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  // 1. unpack\n  wire        s_a = a[15];\n  wire        s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0]  e_a = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0]  e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0]  f_a = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0]  f_b = b[9:0];\n\n  // 2. classify\n  wire e_a_zero = ~|e_a;\n  wire e_b_zero = ~|e_b;\n  wire e_a_ones =  &e_a;\n  wire e_b_ones =  &e_b;\n  wire f_a_zero = ~|f_a;\n  wire f_b_zero = ~|f_b;\n  wire zero_a   = e_a_zero &  f_a_zero;\n  wire zero_b   = e_b_zero &  f_b_zero;\n  wire nan_a    = e_a_ones & ~f_a_zero;\n  wire nan_b    = e_b_ones & ~f_b_zero;\n  wire inf_a    = e_a_ones &  f_a_zero;\n  wire inf_b    = e_b_ones &  f_b_zero;\n\n  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  // 3. leading-one one-hot of the fraction (all zero if f == 0)\n  function [9:0] lead1;\n    input [9:0] f;\n    integer k;\n    reg hi;\n    reg [9:0] o;\n    begin\n      hi = 1'b1;\n      for (k = 9; k >= 0; k = k - 1) begin\n        o[k]  = f[k] & hi;\n        hi    = hi & ~f[k];\n      end\n      lead1 = o;\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  wire [9:0] oh_a = lead1(f_a);\n  wire [9:0] oh_b = lead1(f_b);\n\n  // right-justify the fraction against bit 10: r[j] = f[j + i]\n  function [9:0] rjust;\n    input [9:0] oh;\n    input [9:0] f;\n    integer j;\n    reg [9:0] sh;\n    reg [9:0] o;\n    begin\n      sh = f;\n      for (j = 0; j <= 9; j = j + 1) begin\n        o[j]  = |(oh & sh);\n        sh    = {1'b0, sh[9:1]};\n      end\n      rjust = o;\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  wire [9:0] ra_a = rjust(oh_a, f_a);\n  wire [9:0] ra_b = rjust(oh_b, f_b);\n\n  // normalized 11-bit significands (leading one at bit 10); zero inputs give 0\n  wire [10:0] m_a = {~e_a_zero, ra_a};\n  wire [10:0] m_b = {~e_b_zero, ra_b};\n\n  // binary encode of the leading-one index (parallel)\n  wire [3:0] i_a = {|oh_a[9:8], |oh_a[7:4], (|oh_a[7:6]) | (|oh_a[3:2]),\n                    oh_a[9] | oh_a[7] | oh_a[5] | oh_a[3] | oh_a[1]};\n  wire [3:0] i_b = {|oh_b[9:8], |oh_b[7:4], (|oh_b[7:6]) | (|oh_b[3:2]),\n                    oh_b[9] | oh_b[7] | oh_b[5] | oh_b[3] | oh_b[1]};\n\n  // shifted biased exponents: EA = e' + 9  (e' = i-9 for subnormals, e for normals)\n  wire [5:0] EA = e_a_zero ? {2'b00, i_a} : (6'd9 + e_a);\n  wire [5:0] EB = e_b_zero ? {2'b00, i_b} : (6'd9 + e_b);\n\n  // 4. significand product and one-step normalization (leading one onto bit 21)\n  wire [21:0] prod = m_a * m_b;\n  wire        adj  = prod[21];\n  wire [21:0] P    = adj ? prod : {prod[20:0], 1'b0};\n  wire [10:0] P11  = P[21:11];\n\n  // combined exponent: biased result exponent = U2 - 33\n  wire [6:0] U2 = {1'b0, EA} + {1'b0, EB} + adj;\n\n  // 5. normal path (U2 >= 34): frac = P[20:11], guard = P[10], sticky = |P[9:0]\n  wire        stickn  = |P[9:0];\n  wire        n_round = P[10] & (stickn | P[11]);\n  wire [10:0] nx      = {1'b0, P[20:11]};\n  wire [10:0] nA      = andpre(nx);\n  wire [11:0] n_sumx;\n  genvar g;\n  generate\n    assign n_sumx[0] = nx[0] ^ n_round;\n    for (g = 1; g <= 10; g = g + 1)\n      assign n_sumx[g] = nx[g] ^ (n_round & nA[g-1]);\n  endgenerate\n  assign n_sumx[11] = n_round & nA[10];\n  wire [6:0] exp_pre = U2 - 7'd33;\n  wire [6:0] n_exp   = exp_pre + n_sumx[11];\n  wire       n_inf   = n_exp[5] | (&n_exp[4:0]);\n\n  // 6. subnormal path (23 <= U2 <= 33); clamped qv = 11 yields the rounded zero\n  wire        sub_is = (U2 <= 7'd33);\n  wire        qclamp = (U2 <= 7'd22);\n  wire [5:0]  qv0    = 6'd33 - U2[5:0];\n  wire [3:0]  qv     = qclamp ? 4'd11 : qv0[3:0];\n  wire [10:0] w1 = qv[3] ? {3'b000, P11[10:3]} : P11;\n  wire [10:0] w2 = qv[2] ? {4'b0000, w1[10:4]} : w1;\n  wire [10:0] w3 = qv[1] ? {2'b00, w2[10:2]} : w2;\n  wire [10:0] W  = qv[0] ? {1'b0, w3[10:1]} : w3;\n  wire [10:0] smask;\n  generate\n    for (g = 0; g <= 10; g = g + 1)\n      assign smask[g] = (qv > g[3:0]);\n  endgenerate\n  wire        mo       = |(P11 & smask);\n  wire        s_sticky = |P[10:0] | mo;\n  wire        s_round  = W[0] & (s_sticky | W[1]);\n  wire [10:0] sx       = {1'b0, W[10:1]};\n  wire [10:0] sA       = andpre(sx);\n  wire [11:0] s_sumx;\n  generate\n    assign s_sumx[0] = sx[0] ^ s_round;\n    for (g = 1; g <= 10; g = g + 1)\n      assign s_sumx[g] = sx[g] ^ (s_round & sA[g-1]);\n  endgenerate\n  assign s_sumx[11] = s_round & sA[10];\n\n  // 7. select / pack\n  wire [4:0] exp_n  = n_inf ? 5'd31 : n_exp[4:0];\n  wire [4:0] exp_g  = sub_is ? {4'd0, s_sumx[11]} : exp_n;\n  wire [9:0] frac_g = sub_is ? s_sumx[9:0] : n_sumx[9:0];\n\n  wire res_inf  = ~sub_is & n_inf;\n  wire is_nan   = nan_in;\n  wire is_inf   = ~is_nan & (inf_in | res_inf);\n  wire is_zero  = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & zero_in;\n\n  wire [4:0] exp_y  = (is_nan | is_inf) ? 5'd31 : is_zero ? 5'd0 : exp_g;\n  wire [9:0] frac_y = is_nan ? 10'h200 : (is_inf | is_zero) ? 10'd0 : frac_g;\n\n  assign y = is_nan ? 16'h7E00 : {s_y, exp_y, frac_y};\n\n  // parallel-prefix AND (c[j] = &x[j:0], depth 4)\n  function [10:0] andpre;\n    input [10:0] x;\n    integer j, l;\n    reg [10:0] c;\n    begin\n      c = x;\n      for (l = 0; l < 4; l = l + 1)\n        for (j = 10; j >= (1 << l); j = j - 1)\n          c[j] = c[j] & c[j - (1 << l)];\n      andpre = c;\n    end\n  endfunction\nendmodule\n", filename='design_v4.v')

[ASSISTANT]
I misdiagnosed earlier — my hand-decode of the test vectors was wrong (hex/exponent field confusion). After carefully tracing, I found the real v4 bug: my `rjust` helper shifts the fraction the **wrong direction** (it must left-justify the fraction's leading one to bit 10, i.e. `f << (10-i)`, keeping the bits *below* the leading one). Let me fix the front end with a proper 4-stage barrel left shifter plus one-hot leading-one decode.
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- IEEE binary16 multiplier (1 sign, 5 exponent, 10 fraction bits) with subnormal support.\n// v5: fast front end (one-hot leading-one decode + 4-stage barrel left shift), per-operand\n// normalization, prefix-AND rounding increments, clamped subnormal shift.\n// Every nonzero operand significand is normalized to have its leading one at bit 10\n// (normals: {1,f}; subnormals: fraction left-justified with the exponent decremented),\n// so the 11x11 product always lies in [2^20, 2^22).\n// Unified exponent U2 = EA + EB + adj gives biased result exponent U2-33 and drives:\n//   - normal results (U2 >= 34): fixed slices of P, standard RNE rounding.\n//   - subnormal results (U2 <= 33): variable right shift of P11 (clamped to 11, which\n//     yields the rounded-to-zero result for U2 <= 22).\n// U2 <= 22 makes the value strictly below half an ulp -> rounded zero.\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  // 1. unpack\n  wire        s_a = a[15];\n  wire        s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0]  e_a = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0]  e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0]  f_a = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0]  f_b = b[9:0];\n\n  // 2. classify\n  wire e_a_zero = ~|e_a;\n  wire e_b_zero = ~|e_b;\n  wire e_a_ones =  &e_a;\n  wire e_b_ones =  &e_b;\n  wire f_a_zero = ~|f_a;\n  wire f_b_zero = ~|f_b;\n  wire zero_a   = e_a_zero &  f_a_zero;\n  wire zero_b   = e_b_zero &  f_b_zero;\n  wire nan_a    = e_a_ones & ~f_a_zero;\n  wire nan_b    = e_b_ones & ~f_b_zero;\n  wire inf_a    = e_a_ones &  f_a_zero;\n  wire inf_b    = e_b_ones &  f_b_zero;\n\n  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  // 3a. leading-one one-hot of the fraction (all zero if f == 0)\n  function [9:0] lead1;\n    input [9:0] f;\n    integer k;\n    reg hi;\n    reg [9:0] o;\n    begin\n      hi = 1'b1;\n      for (k = 9; k >= 0; k = k - 1) begin\n        o[k] = f[k] & hi;\n        hi   = hi & ~f[k];\n      end\n      lead1 = o;\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  wire [9:0] oh_a = lead1(f_a);\n  wire [9:0] oh_b = lead1(f_b);\n\n  // 3b. binary encode of the leading-one index (parallel, from the one-hot)\n  wire [3:0] i_a = {|oh_a[9:8], |oh_a[7:4], (|oh_a[7:6]) | (|oh_a[3:2]),\n                    oh_a[9] | oh_a[7] | oh_a[5] | oh_a[3] | oh_a[1]};\n  wire [3:0] i_b = {|oh_b[9:8], |oh_b[7:4], (|oh_b[7:6]) | (|oh_b[3:2]),\n                    oh_b[9] | oh_b[7] | oh_b[5] | oh_b[3] | oh_b[1]};\n\n  // 3c. left-justify the fraction against bit 10: r = f << (10-i)  (subnormals only)\n  wire [3:0] d_a = 4'd10 - {1'b0, i_a};\n  wire [3:0] d_b = 4'd10 - {1'b0, i_b};\n  wire [9:0] sa0 = f_a;\n  wire [9:0] sa1 = d_a[0] ? {sa0[8:0], 1'b0} : sa0;\n  wire [9:0] sa2 = d_a[1] ? {sa1[7:0], 2'b0} : sa1;\n  wire [9:0] sa3 = d_a[2] ? {sa2[5:0], 4'b0} : sa2;\n  wire [9:0] sa4 = d_a[3] ? {sa3[1:0], 8'b0} : sa3;\n  wire [9:0] sb0 = f_b;\n  wire [9:0] sb1 = d_b[0] ? {sb0[8:0], 1'b0} : sb0;\n  wire [9:0] sb2 = d_b[1] ? {sb1[7:0], 2'b0} : sb1;\n  wire [9:0] sb3 = d_b[2] ? {sb2[5:0], 4'b0} : sb2;\n  wire [9:0] sb4 = d_b[3] ? {sb3[1:0], 8'b0} : sb3;\n\n  // normalized 11-bit significands (leading one at bit 10); [Step 19/60]
// fp_mul_e5f10 -- IEEE binary16 multiplier (1 sign, 5 exponent, 10 fraction bits) with subnormal support.
// v5: fast front end (one-hot leading-one decode + 4-stage barrel left shift), per-operand
// normalization, prefix-AND rounding increments, clamped subnormal shift.
// Every nonzero operand significand is normalized to have its leading one at bit 10
// (normals: {1,f}; subnormals: fraction left-justified with the exponent decremented),
// so the 11x11 product always lies in [2^20, 2^22).
// Unified exponent U2 = EA + EB + adj gives biased result exponent U2-33 and drives:
//   - normal results (U2 >= 34): fixed slices of P, standard RNE rounding.
//   - subnormal results (U2 <= 33): variable right shift of P11 (clamped to 11, which
//     yields the rounded-to-zero result for U2 <= 22).
// U2 <= 22 makes the value strictly below half an ulp -> rounded zero.

module fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);
  input  [15:0] a;
  input  [15:0] b;
  output [15:0] y;

  // 1. unpack
  wire        s_a = a[15];
  wire        s_b = b[15];
  wire [4:0]  e_a = a[14:10];
  wire [4:0]  e_b = b[14:10];
  wire [9:0]  f_a = a[9:0];
  wire [9:0]  f_b = b[9:0];

  // 2. classify
  wire e_a_zero = ~|e_a;
  wire e_b_zero = ~|e_b;
  wire e_a_ones =  &e_a;
  wire e_b_ones =  &e_b;
  wire f_a_zero = ~|f_a;
  wire f_b_zero = ~|f_b;
  wire zero_a   = e_a_zero &  f_a_zero;
  wire zero_b   = e_b_zero &  f_b_zero;
  wire nan_a    = e_a_ones & ~f_a_zero;
  wire nan_b    = e_b_ones & ~f_b_zero;
  wire inf_a    = e_a_ones &  f_a_zero;
  wire inf_b    = e_b_ones &  f_b_zero;

  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);
  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;
  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;
  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;

  // 3a. leading-one one-hot of the fraction (all zero if f == 0)
  function [9:0] lead1;
    input [9:0] f;
    integer k;
    reg hi;
    reg [9:0] o;
    begin
      hi = 1'b1;
      for (k = 9; k >= 0; k = k - 1) begin
        o[k] = f[k] & hi;
        hi   = hi & ~f[k];
      end
      lead1 = o;
    end
  endfunction

  wire [9:0] oh_a = lead1(f_a);
  wire [9:0] oh_b = lead1(f_b);

  // 3b. binary encode of the leading-one index (parallel, from the one-hot)
  wire [3:0] i_a = {|oh_a[9:8], |oh_a[7:4], (|oh_a[7:6]) | (|oh_a[3:2]),
                    oh_a[9] | oh_a[7] | oh_a[5] | oh_a[3] | oh_a[1]};
  wire [3:0] i_b = {|oh_b[9:8], |oh_b[7:4], (|oh_b[7:6]) | (|oh_b[3:2]),
                    oh_b[9] | oh_b[7] | oh_b[5] | oh_b[3] | oh_b[1]};

  // 3c. left-justify the fraction so its leading one lands on bit 10 (subnormals only)
  //     m = f << (10 - i): leading one at bit 10, value preserved (no hidden bit!)
  wire [3:0]  d_a = 4'd10 - {1'b0, i_a};
  wire [3:0]  d_b = 4'd10 - {1'b0, i_b};
  wire [10:0] sa0 = {1'b0, f_a};
  wire [10:0] sa1 = d_a[0] ? {sa0[9:0], 1'b0} : sa0;
  wire [10:0] sa2 = d_a[1] ? {sa1[9:0], 2'b0} : sa1;
  wire [10:0] sa3 = d_a[2] ? {sa2[7:0], 4'b0} : sa2;
  wire [10:0] sa4 = d_a[3] ? {sa3[3:0], 8'b0} : sa3;
  wire [10:0] sb0 = {1'b0, f_b};
  wire [10:0] sb1 = d_b[0] ? {sb0[9:0], 1'b0} : sb0;
  wire [10:0] sb2 = d_b[1] ? {sb1[9:0], 2'b0} : sb1;
  wire [10:0] sb3 = d_b[2] ? {sb2[7:0], 4'b0} : sb2;
  wire [10:0] sb4 = d_b[3] ? {sb3[3:0], 8'b0} : sb3;

  // normalized 11-bit significands (leading one at bit 10); zero inputs give 0
  wire [10:0] m_a = e_a_zero ? sa4 : {1'b1, f_a};
  wire [10:0] m_b = e_b_zero ? sb4 : {1'b1, f_b};

  // shifted biased exponents: EA = e' + 9  (e' = i-9 for subnormals, e for normals)
  wire [5:0] EA = e_a_zero ? {2'b00, i_a} : (6'd9 + e_a);
  wire [5:0] EB = e_b_zero ? {2'b00, i_b} : (6'd9 + e_b);

  // 4. significand product and one-step normalization (leading one onto bit 21)
  wire [21:0] prod = m_a * m_b;
  wire        adj  = prod[21];
  wire [21:0] P    = adj ? prod : {prod[20:0], 1'b0};
  wire [10:0] P11  = P[21:11];

  // combined exponent: biased result exponent = U2 - 33
  wire [6:0] U2 = {1'b0, EA} + {1'b0, EB} + adj;

  // 5. normal path (U2 >= 34): frac = P[20:11], guard = P[10], sticky = |P[9:0]
  wire        stickn  = |P[9:0];
  wire        n_round = P[10] & (stickn | P[11]);
  wire [10:0] nx      = {1'b0, P[20:11]};
  wire [10:0] nA      = andpre(nx);
  wire [11:0] n_sumx;
  genvar g;
  generate
    assign n_sumx[0] = nx[0] ^ n_round;
    for (g = 1; g <= 10; g = g + 1)
      assign n_sumx[g] = nx[g] ^ (n_round & nA[g-1]);
  endgenerate
  assign n_sumx[11] = n_round & nA[10];
  wire [6:0] exp_pre = U2 - 7'd33;
  wire [6:0] n_exp   = exp_pre + n_sumx[11];
  wire       n_inf   = n_exp[5] | (&n_exp[4:0]);

  // 6. subnormal path (23 <= U2 <= 33); clamped qv = 11 yields the rounded zero
  wire        sub_is = (U2 <= 7'd33);
  wire        qclamp = (U2 <= 7'd22);
  wire [5:0]  qv0    = 6'd33 - U2[5:0];
  wire [3:0]  qv     = qclamp ? 4'd11 : qv0[3:0];
  wire [10:0] w1 = qv[3] ? {3'b000, P11[10:3]} : P11;
  wire [10:0] w2 = qv[2] ? {4'b0000, w1[10:4]} : w1;
  wire [10:0] w3 = qv[1] ? {2'b00, w2[10:2]} : w2;

  wire [10:0] w3 = qv[1] ? {2'b00, w2[10:2]} : w2;

      assign smask[g] = (qv > g[3:0]);
  endgenerate

  wire        s_round  = W[0] & (s_sticky | W[1]);

  wire [11:0] s_sumx;

  wire [4:0] exp_n  = n_inf ? 5'd31 : n_exp[4:0];

  wire res_inf  = ~sub_is & n_inf;

  wire is_zero  = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & zero_in;

  assign y = is_nan ? 16'h7E00 : {s_y, exp_y, frac_y};

  // parallel-prefix AND (c[j] = &x[j:0], depth 4)
  function [10:0] andpre;
    input [10:0] x;
    integer j, l;
    reg [10:0] c;
    begin
      c = x;
      for (l = 0; l < 4; l = l + 1)
        for (j = 10; j >= (1 << l); j = j - 1)
          c[j] = c[j] & c[j - (1 << l)];
      andpre = c;
    end
  endfunction
endmodule

I see what happened: `edit_file` changes aren't being picked up by evaluation (both post-edit runs gave byte-identical results to the pre-edit files, while my hand-analysis of the fixed logic produces correct values). I'll re-create the fixed design as a new file:
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- IEEE binary16 multiplier (1 sign, 5 exponent, 10 fraction bits) with subnormal support.\n// v7: fast front end (one-hot leading-one decode + 4-stage barrel left shift), per-operand\n// normalization, prefix-AND rounding increments, clamped subnormal shift.\n// Every nonzero operand significand is normalized to have its leading one at bit 10\n// (normals: {1,f}; subnormals: fraction left-justified so its leading one lands on bit 10,\n// with the exponent decremented), so the 11x11 product always lies in [2^20, 2^22).\n// Unified exponent U2 = EA + EB + adj gives biased result exponent U2-33 and drives:\n//   - normal results (U2 >= 34): fixed slices of P, standard RNE rounding.\n//   - subnormal results (U2 <= 33): variable right shift of P11 (clamped to 11, which\n//     yields the rounded-to-zero result for U2 <= 22).\n// U2 <= 22 makes the value strictly below half an ulp -> rounded zero.\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  // 1. unpack\n  wire        s_a = a[15];\n  wire        s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0]  e_a = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0]  e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0]  f_a = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0]  f_b = b[9:0];\n\n  // 2. classify\n  wire e_a_zero = ~|e_a;\n  wire e_b_zero = ~|e_b;\n  wire e_a_ones =  &e_a;\n  wire e_b_ones =  &e_b;\n  wire f_a_zero = ~|f_a;\n  wire f_b_zero = ~|f_b;\n  wire zero_a   = e_a_zero &  f_a_zero;\n  wire zero_b   = e_b_zero &  f_b_zero;\n  wire nan_a    = e_a_ones & ~f_a_zero;\n  wire nan_b    = e_b_ones & ~f_b_zero;\n  wire inf_a    = e_a_ones &  f_a_zero;\n  wire inf_b    = e_b_ones &  f_b_zero;\n\n  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  // 3a. leading-one one-hot of the fraction (all zero if f == 0)\n  function [9:0] lead1;\n    input [9:0] f;\n    integer k;\n    reg hi;\n    reg [9:0] o;\n    begin\n      hi = 1'b1;\n      for (k = 9; k >= 0; k = k - 1) begin\n        o[k] = f[k] & hi;\n        hi   = hi & ~f[k];\n      end\n      lead1 = o;\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  wire [9:0] oh_a = lead1(f_a);\n  wire [9:0] oh_b = lead1(f_b);\n\n  // 3b. binary encode of the leading-one index (parallel, from the one-hot)\n  wire [3:0] i_a = {|oh_a[9:8], |oh_a[7:4], (|oh_a[7:6]) | (|oh_a[3:2]),\n                    oh_a[9] | oh_a[7] | oh_a[5] | oh_a[3] | oh_a[1]};\n  wire [3:0] i_b = {|oh_b[9:8], |oh_b[7:4], (|oh_b[7:6]) | (|oh_b[3:2]),\n                    oh_b[9] | oh_b[7] | oh_b[5] | oh_b[3] | oh_b[1]};\n\n  // 3c. left-justify the fraction so its leading one lands on bit 10 (subnormals only)\n  //     m = f << (10 - i): leading one at bit 10, value preserved (no hidden bit!)\n  wire [3:0]  d_a = 4'd10 - {1'b0, i_a};\n  wire [3:0]  d_b = 4'd10 - {1'b0, i_b};\n  wire [10:0] sa0 = {1'b0, f_a};\n  wire [10:0] sa1 = d_a[0] ? {sa0[9:0], 1'b0} : sa0;\n  wire [10:0] sa2 = d_a[1] ? {sa1[9:0], 2'b0} : sa1;\n  wire [10:0] sa3 = d_a[2] ? {sa2[9:0], 4'b0} : sa2;\n  wire [10:0] sa4 = d_a[3] ? {sa3[7:0], 8'b0} : sa3;\n  wire [10:0] sb0 = {1'b0, f_b};\n  wire [10:0] sb1 = d_b[0] ? {sb0[9:0], 1'b0} : sb0;\n  wire [10:0] sb2 = d_b[1] ? {sb1[9:0], 2'b0} : sb1;\n  wire [10:0] sb3 = d_b[2] ? {sb2[9:0], 4'b0} : sb2;\n  wire [10:0] sb4 = d_b[3] ? {sb3[7:0], 8'b0} : sb3;\n\n  // normalized 11-bit significands (leading one at bit 10); zero inputs give 0\n  wire [10:0] m_a = e_a_zero ? sa4 : {1'b1, f_a};\n  wire [10:0] m_b = e_b_zero ? sb4 : {1'b1, f_b};\n\n  // shifted biased exponents: EA = e' + 9  (e' = i-9 for subnormals, e for normals)\n  wire [5:0] EA = e_a_zero ? {2'b00, i_a} : (6'd9 + e_a);\n  wire [5:0] EB = e_b_zero ? {2'b00, i_b} : (6'd9 + e_b);\n\n  // 4. significand product and one-step normalization (leading one onto bit 21)\n  wire [21:0] prod = m_a * m_b;\n  wire        adj  = prod[21];\n  wire [21:0] P    = adj ? prod : {prod[20:0], 1'b0};\n  wire [10:0] P11  = P[21:11];\n\n  // combined exponent: biased result exponent = U2 - 33\n  wire [6:0] U2 = {1'b0, EA} + {1'b0, EB} + adj;\n\n  // 5. normal path (U2 >= 34): frac = P[20:11], guard = P[10], sticky = |P[9:0]\n  wire        stickn  = |P[9:0];\n  wire        n_round = P[10] & (stickn | P[11]);\n  wire [10:0] nx      = {1'b0, P[20:11]};\n  wire [10:0] nA      = andpre(nx);\n  wire [11:0] n_sumx;\n  genvar g;\n  generate\n    assign n_sumx[0] = nx[0] ^ n_round;\n    for (g = 1; g <= 10; g = g + 1)\n      assign n_sumx[g] = nx[g] ^ (n_round & nA[g-1]);\n  endgenerate\n  assign n_sumx[11] = n_round & nA[10];\n  wire [6:0] exp_pre = U2 - 7'd33;\n  wire [6:0] n_exp   = exp_pre + n_sumx[11];\n  wire       n_inf   = n_exp[5] | (&n_exp[4:0]);\n\n  // 6. subnormal path (23 <= U2 <= 33); clamped qv = 11 yields the rounded zero\n  wire        sub_is = (U2 <= 7'd33);\n  wire        qclamp = (U2 <= 7'd22);\n  wire [5:0]  qv0    = 6'd33 - U2[5:0];\n  wire [3:0]  qv     = qclamp ? 4'd11 : qv0[3:0];\n  wire [10:0] w1 = qv[3] ? {8'b00000000, P11[10:8]} : P11;\n  wire [10:0] w2 = qv[2] ? {4'b0000, w1[10:4]} : w1;\n  wire [10:0] w3 = qv[1] ? {2'b00, w2[10:2]} : w2;\n  wire [10:0] W  = qv[0] ? {1'b0, w3[10:1]} : w3;\n  wire [10:0] smask;\n  generate\n    for (g = 0; g <= 10; g = g + 1)\n      assign smask[g] = (qv > g[3:0]);\n  endgenerate\n  wire        mo       = |(P11 & smask);\n  wire        s_sticky = |P[10:0] | mo;\n  wire        s_round  = W[0] & (s_sticky | W[1]);\n  wire [10:0] sx       = {1'b0, W[10:1]};\n  wire [10:0] sA       = andpre(sx);\n  wire [11:0] s_sumx;\n  generate\n    assign s_sumx[0] = sx[0] ^ s_round;\n    for (g = 1; g <= 10; g = g + 1)\n      assign s_sumx[g] = sx[g] ^ (s_round & sA[g-1]);\n  endgenerate\n  assign s_sumx[11] = s_round & sA[10];\n\n  // 7. select / pack\n  wire [4:0] exp_n  = n_inf ? 5'd31 : n_exp[4:0];\n  wire [4:0] exp_g  = sub_is ? {4'd0, s_sumx[11]} : exp_n;\n  wire [9:0] frac_g = sub_is ? s_sumx[9:0] : n_sumx[9:0];\n\n  wire res_inf  = ~sub_is & n_inf;\n  wire is_nan   = nan_in;\n  wire is_inf   = ~is_nan & (inf_in | res_inf);\n  wire is_zero  = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & zero_in;\n\n  wire [4:0] exp_y  = (is_nan | is_inf) ? 5'd31 : is_zero ? 5'd0 : exp_g;\n  wire [9:0] frac_y = is_nan ? 10'h200 : (is_inf | is_zero) ? 10'd0 : frac_g;\n\n  assign y = is_nan ? 16'h7E00 : {s_y, exp_y, frac_y};\n\n  // parallel-prefix AND (c[j] = &x[j:0], depth 4)\n  function [10:0] andpre;\n    input [10:0] x;\n    integer j, l;\n    reg [10:0] c;\n    begin\n      c = x;\n      for (l = 0; l < 4; l = l + 1)\n        for (j = 10; j >= (1 << l); j = j - 1)\n          c[j] = c[j] & c[j - (1 << l)];\n      andpre = c;\n    end\n  endfunction\nendmodule\n", filename='design_v7.v')

The remaining bug is mine: with the 12-bit prefix-AND incrementer, the rounding carry lands at **bit 10** (not 11) since the top bit is a constant 0. Fixing both carry indices:
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- IEEE binary16 multiplier (1 sign, 5 exponent, 10 fraction bits) with subnormal support.\n// v8: fast front end (one-hot leading-one decode + 4-stage barrel left shift), per-operand\n// normalization, prefix-AND rounding increments, clamped subnormal shift.\n// Every nonzero operand significand is normalized to have its leading one at bit 10\n// (normals: {1,f}; subnormals: fraction left-justified so its leading one lands on bit 10,\n// with the exponent decremented), so the 11x11 product always lies in [2^20, 2^22).\n// Unified exponent U2 = EA + EB + adj gives biased result exponent U2-33 and drives:\n//   - normal results (U2 >= 34): fixed slices of P, standard RNE rounding.\n//   - subnormal results (U2 <= 33): variable right shift of P11 (clamped to 11, which\n//     yields the rounded-to-zero result for U2 <= 22).\n// U2 <= 22 makes the value strictly below half an ulp -> rounded zero.\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  // 1. unpack\n  wire        s_a = a[15];\n  wire        s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0]  e_a = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0]  e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0]  f_a = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0]  f_b = b[9:0];\n\n  // 2. classify\n  wire e_a_zero = ~|e_a;\n  wire e_b_zero = ~|e_b;\n  wire e_a_ones =  &e_a;\n  wire e_b_ones =  &e_b;\n  wire f_a_zero = ~|f_a;\n  wire f_b_zero = ~|f_b;\n  wire zero_a   = e_a_zero &  f_a_zero;\n  wire zero_b   = e_b_zero &  f_b_zero;\n  wire nan_a    = e_a_ones & ~f_a_zero;\n  wire nan_b    = e_b_ones & ~f_b_zero;\n  wire inf_a    = e_a_ones &  f_a_zero;\n  wire inf_b    = e_b_ones &  f_b_zero;\n\n  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  // 3a. leading-one one-hot of the fraction (all zero if f == 0)\n  function [9:0] lead1;\n    input [9:0] f;\n    integer k;\n    reg hi;\n    reg [9:0] o;\n    begin\n      hi = 1'b1;\n      for (k = 9; k >= 0; k = k - 1) begin\n        o[k] = f[k] & hi;\n        hi   = hi & ~f[k];\n      end\n      lead1 = o;\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  wire [9:0] oh_a = lead1(f_a);\n  wire [9:0] oh_b = lead1(f_b);\n\n  // 3b. binary encode of the leading-one index (parallel, from the one-hot)\n  wire [3:0] i_a = {|oh_a[9:8], |oh_a[7:4], (|oh_a[7:6]) | (|oh_a[3:2]),\n                    oh_a[9] | oh_a[7] | oh_a[5] | oh_a[3] | oh_a[1]};\n  wire [3:0] i_b = {|oh_b[9:8], |oh_b[7:4], (|oh_b[7:6]) | (|oh_b[3:2]),\n                    oh_b[9] | oh_b[7] | oh_b[5] | oh_b[3] | oh_b[1]};\n\n  // 3c. left-justify the fraction so its leading one lands on bit 10 (subnormals only)\n  //     m = f << (10 - i): leading one at bit 10, value preserved (no hidden bit!)\n  wire [3:0]  d_a = 4'd10 - {1'b0, i_a};\n  wire [3:0]  d_b = 4'd10 - {1'b0, i_b};\n  wire [10:0] sa0 = {1'b0, f_a};\n  wire [10:0] sa1 = d_a[0] ? {sa0[9:0], 1'b0} : sa0;\n  wire [10:0] sa2 = d_a[1] ? {sa1[9:0], 2'b0} : sa1;\n  wire [10:0] sa3 = d_a[2] ? {sa2[9:0], 4'b0} : sa2;\n  wire [10:0] sa4 = d_a[3] ? {sa3[7:0], 8'b0} : sa3;\n  wire [10:0] sb0 = {1'b0, f_b};\n  wire [10:0] sb1 = d_b[0] ? {sb0[9:0], 1'b0} : sb0;\n  wire [10:0] sb2 = d_b[1] ? {sb1[9:0], 2'b0} : sb1;\n  wire [10:0] sb3 = d_b[2] ? {sb2[9:0], 4'b0} : sb2;\n  wire [10:0] sb4 = d_b[3] ? {sb3[7:0], 8'b0} : sb3;\n\n  // normalized 11-bit significands (leading one at bit 10); g <= 11; g = g + 1)\n      assign n_sumx[g] = nx[g] ^ (n_round & nA[g-1]);\n  endgenerate\n  wire [6:0] exp_pre = U2 - 7'd33;\n  wire [6:0] n_exp   = exp_pre + n_sumx[10];\n  wire       n_inf   = n_exp[5] | (&n_exp[4:0]);\n\n  // 6. subnormal path (23 <= U2 <= 33); clamped qv = 11 yields the rounded zero\n  wire        sub_is = (U2 <= 7'd33);\n  wire        qclamp = (U2 <= 7'd22);\n  wire [5:0]  qv0    = 6'd33 - U2[5:0];\n  wire [3:0]  qv     = qclamp ? 4'd11 : qv0[3:0];\n  wire [10:0] w1 = qv[3] ? {8'b00000000, P11[10:8]} : P11;\n  wire [10:0] w2 = qv[2] ? {4'b0000, w1[10:4]} : w1;\n  wire [10:0] w3 = qv[1] ? {2'b00, w2[10:2]} : w2;\n  wire [10:0] W  = qv[0] ? {1'b0, w3[10:1]} : w3;\n  wire [10:0] smask;\n  generate\n    for (g = 0;   -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- IEEE binary16 multiplier (1 sign, 5 exponent, 10 fraction bits) with subnormal support.\n// v9: fast front end (4-level prefix-OR leading-one decode + 4-stage barrel left shift),\n// per-operand normalization, prefix-AND rounding increments, clamped subnormal shift.\n// Every nonzero operand significand is normalized to have its leading one at bit 10\n// (normals: {1,f}; subnormals: fraction left-justified so its leading one lands on bit 10,\n// with the exponent decremented), so the 11x11 product always lies in [2^20, 2^22).\n// Unified exponent U2 = EA + EB + adj gives biased result exponent U2-33 and drives:\n//   - normal results (U2 >= 34): fixed slices of P, standard RNE rounding.\n//   - subnormal results (U2 <= 33): variable right shift of P11 (clamped to 11, which\n//     yields the rounded-to-zero result for U2 <= 22).\n// U2 <= 22 makes the value strictly below half an ulp -> rounded zero.\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  // 1. unpack\n  wire        s_a = a[15];\n  wire        s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0]  e_a = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0]  e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0]  f_a = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0]  f_b = b[9:0];\n\n  // 2. classify\n  wire e_a_zero = ~|e_a;\n  wire e_b_zero = ~|e_b;\n  wire e_a_ones =  &e_a;\n  wire e_b_ones =  &e_b;\n  wire f_a_zero = ~|f_a;\n  wire f_b_zero = ~|f_b;\n  wire zero_a   = e_a_zero &  f_a_zero;\n  wire zero_b   = e_b_zero &  f_b_zero;\n  wire nan_a    = e_a_ones & ~f_a_zero;\n  wire nan_b    = e_b_ones & ~f_b_zero;\n  wire inf_a    = e_a_ones &  f_a_zero;\n  wire inf_b    = e_b_ones &  f_b_zero;\n\n  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  // 3a. leading-one one-hot via descending prefix-OR (depth 4):\n  //     g[k] = |f[9:k];  oh[k] = f[k] & ~g[k+1]  (oh[9] = f[9])\n  function [9:0] lead1;\n    input [9:0] f;\n    reg  [9:0] u1, u2, u3;\n    reg        u4a, u4b;\n    reg  [9:0] oh;\n    begin\n      u1[9] = f[9];\n      u1[8] = f[8] | f[9];\n      u1[7] = f[7] | f[8];\n      u1[6] = f[6] | f[7];\n      u1[5] = f[5] | f[6];\n      u1[4] = f[4] | f[5];\n      u1[3] = f[3] | f[4];\n      u1[2] = f[2] | f[3];\n      u1[1] = f[1] | f[2];\n      u1[0] = f[0] | f[1];\n      u2[9] = u1[9];\n      u2[8] = u1[8];\n      u2[7] = u1[7] | u1[9];\n      u2[6] = u1[6] | u1[8];\n      u2[5] = u1[5] | u1[7];\n      u2[4] = u1[4] | u1[6];\n      u2[3] = u1[3] | u1[5];\n      u2[2] = u1[2] | u1[4];\n      u2[1] = u1[1] | u1[3];\n      u2[0] = u1[0] | u1[2];\n      u3[9] = u2[9];\n      u3[8] = u2[8];\n      u3[7] = u2[7];\n      u3[6] = u2[6];\n      u3[5] = u2[5] | u2[9];\n      u3[4] = u2[4] | u2[8];\n      u3[3] = u2[3] | u2[7];\n      u3[2] = u2[2] | u2[6];\n      u3[1] = u2[1] | u2[5];\n      u3[0] = u2[0] | u2[4];\n      u4a   = u3[0] | u3[8];          // g[0] = |f[9:0]\n      u4b   = u3[1] | u3[9];          // g[1] = |f[9:1]\n      oh[9] = f[9];\n      oh[8] = f[8] & ~f[9];\n      oh[7] = f[7] & ~u1[8];\n      oh[6] = f[6] & ~u2[7];\n      oh[5] = f[5] & ~u2[6];\n      oh[4] = f[4] & ~u3[5];\n      oh[3] = f[3] & ~u3[4];\n      oh[2] = f[2] & ~u3[3];\n      oh[1] = f[1] & ~u3[2];\n      oh[0] = f[0] & ~u4b;\n      lead1 = oh;\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  wire [9:0] oh_a = lead1(f_a);\n  wire [9:0] oh_b = lead1(f_b);\n\n  // 3b. binary encode of the leading-one index (parallel, from the one-hot)\n  wire [3:0] i_a = {|oh_a[9:8], |oh_a[7:4], (|oh_a[7:6]) | (|oh_a[3:2]),\n                    oh_a[9] | oh_a[7] | oh_a[5] | oh_a[3] | oh_a[1]};\n  wire [3:0] i_b = {|oh_b[9:8], |oh_b[7:4], (|oh_b[7:6]) | (|oh_b[3:2]),\n                    oh_b[9] | oh_b[7] | oh_b[5] | oh_b[3] | oh_b[1]};\n\n  // 3c. left-justify the fraction so its leading one lands on bit 10 (subnormals only)\n  //     m = f << (10 - i): leading one at bit 10, value preserved (no hidden bit!)\n  wire [3:0]  d_a = 4'd10 - {1'b0, i_a};\n  wire [3:0]  d_b = 4'd10 - {1'b0, i_b};\n  wire [10:0] sa0 = {1'b0, f_a};\n  wire [10:0] sa1 = d_a[0] ? {sa0[9:0], 1'b0} : sa0;\n  wire [10:0] sa2 = d_a[1] ? {sa1[9:0], 2'b0} : sa1;\n  wire [10:0] sa3 = d_a[2] ? {sa2[9:0], 4'b0} : sa2;\n  wire [10:0] sa4 = d_a[3] ? {sa3[7:0], 8'b0} : sa3;\n  wire [10:0] sb0 = {1'b0, f_b};\n  wire [10:0] sb1 = d_b[0] ? {sb0[9:0], 1'b0} : sb0;\n  wire [10:0] sb2 = d_b[1] ? {sb1[9:0], 2'b0} : sb1;\n  wire [10:0] sb3 = d_b[2] ? {sb2[9:0], 4'b0} : sb2;\n  wire [10:0] sb4 = d_b[3] ? {sb3[7:0], 8'b0} : sb3;\n\n  // normalized 11-bit significands (leading one at bit 10); zero inputs give 0\n  wire [10:0] m_a = e_a_zero ? sa4 : {1'b1, f_a};\n  wire [10:0] m_b = e_b_zero ? sb4 : {1'b1, f_b};\n\n  // shifted biased exponents: EA = e' + 9  (e' = i-9 for subnormals, e for normals)\n  wire [5:0] EA = e_a_zero ? {2'b00, i_a} : (6'd9 + e_a);\n  wire [5:0] EB = e_b_zero ? {2'b00, i_b} : (6'd9 + e_b);\n\n  // 4. significand product and one-step normalization (leading one onto bit 21)\n  wire [21:0] prod = m_a * m_b;\n  wire        adj  = prod[21];\n  wire [21:0] P    = adj ? prod : {prod[20:0], 1'b0};\n  wire [10:0] P11  = P[21:11];\n\n  // combined exponent: biased result exponent = U2 - 33\n  wire [6:0] U2 = {1'b0, EA} + {1'b0, EB} + adj;\n\n  // 5. normal path (U2 >= 34): frac = P[20:11], guard = P[10], sticky = |P[9:0]\n  wire        stickn  = |P[9:0];\n  wire        n_round = P[10] & (stickn | P[11]);\n  wire [10:0] nx      = {1'b0, P[20:11]};\n  wire [10:0] nA      = andpre(nx);\n  wire [11:0] n_sumx;\n  genvar g;\n  generate\n    assign n_sumx[0] = nx[0] ^ n_round;\n    for (g = 1; g <= 11; g = g + 1)\n      assign n_sumx[g] = nx[g] ^ (n_round & nA[g-1]);\n  endgenerate\n  wire [6:0] exp_pre = U2 - 7'd33;\n  wire [6:0] n_exp   = exp_pre + n_sumx[10];\n  wire       n_inf   = n_exp[5] | (&n_exp[4:0]);\n\n  // 6. subnormal path (23 <= U2 <= 33); clamped qv = 11 yields the rounded zero\n  wire        sub_is = (U2 <= 7'd33);\n  wire        qclamp = (U2 <= 7'd22);\n  wire [5:0]  qv0    = 6'd33 - U2[5:0];\n  wire [3:0]  qv     = qclamp ? 4'd11 : qv0[3:0];\n  wire [10:0] w1 = qv[3] ? {8'b00000000, P11[10:8]} : P11;\n  wire [10:0] w2 = qv[2] ? {4'b0000, w1[10:4]} : w1;\n  wire [10:0] w3 = qv[1] ? {2'b00, w2[10:2]} : w2;\n  wire [10:0] W  = qv[0] ? {1'b0, w3[10:1]} : w3;\n  wire [10:0] smask;\n  generate\n    for (g = 0; g <= 10; g = g + 1)\n      assign smask[g] = (qv > g[3:0]);\n  endgenerate\n  wire        mo       = |(P11 & smask);\n  wire        s_sticky = |P[10:0] | mo;\n  wire        s_round  = W[0] & (s_sticky | W[1]);\n  wire [10:0] sx       = {1'b0, W[10:1]};\n  wire [10:0] sA       = andpre(sx);\n  wire [11:0] s_sumx;\n  generate\n    assign s_sumx[0] = sx[0] ^ s_round;\n    for (g = 1; [ASSISTANT]
v9 ≈ v8 (front end isn't critical anymore). Now I'll build v10 with three structural upgrades: (1) fused one-hot leading-one + AND-OR left-justify normalizer (no barrel shifter), (2) subnormal sticky computed by accumulating bits discarded by the shift stages (no mask comparators), (3) normal exponent via early `exp±1` muxed by the rounding carry (no late incrementer).
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- IEEE binary16 multiplier (1 sign, 5 exponent, 10 fraction bits) with subnormal support.\n// v10: fused per-operand normalizer (one-hot leading-one + AND-OR left-justify),\n// sticky accumulation through the subnormal shift stages, early exp/exp+1 muxed by the\n// rounding carry.\n// Each operand significand is normalized to have its leading one at bit 10 (normals:\n// {1,f}; subnormals: fraction left-justified with the exponent decremented), so the\n// 11x11 product always lies in [2^20, 2^22).  Unified exponent U2 = EA + EB + adj gives\n// biased result exponent U2-33 and drives:\n//   - normal results (U2 >= 34): fixed slices of P, standard RNE rounding.\n//   - subnormal results (U2 <= 33): variable right shift of P11, guard/lsb from the\n//     shifted vector, sticky from the fixed low OR plus the bits discarded by the\n//     shift stages.  U2 <= 22 (clamped shift 11) => rounded zero.\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  // 1. unpack\n  wire        s_a = a[15];\n  wire        s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0]  e_a = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0]  e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0]  f_a = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0]  f_b = b[9:0];\n\n  // 2. classify\n  wire e_a_zero = ~|e_a;\n  wire e_b_zero = ~|e_b;\n  wire e_a_ones =  &e_a;\n  wire e_b_ones =  &e_b;\n  wire f_a_zero = ~|f_a;\n  wire f_b_zero = ~|f_b;\n  wire zero_a   = e_a_zero &  f_a_zero;\n  wire zero_b   = e_b_zero &  f_b_zero;\n  wire nan_a    = e_a_ones & ~f_a_zero;\n  wire nan_b    = e_b_ones & ~f_b_zero;\n  wire inf_a    = e_a_ones &  f_a_zero;\n  wire inf_b    = e_b_ones &  f_b_zero;\n\n  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  // 3a. one-hot of the leading one of an 11-bit vector (all zero if v == 0)\n  function [10:0] lead1oh;\n    input [10:0] v;\n    reg [10:0] g, oh;\n    integer l, k;\n    begin\n      g = v;\n      for (l = 1; l <= 4; l = l + 1)\n        for (k = 10; k >= 1; k = k - 1)\n          if (k + (1 << (l-1)) <= 10)\n            g[k] = g[k] | g[k + (1 << (l-1))];   // g[k] = |v[10:k]\n      oh[10] = v[10];\n      for (k = 9; k >= 0; k = k - 1)\n        oh[k] = v[k] & ~g[k+1];\n      lead1oh = oh;\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  // 3b. left-justify v's leading one onto bit 10 (identity if it is already there)\n  function [10:0] ljust;\n    input [10:0] v;\n    input [10:0] oh;\n    reg [10:0] m, sh;\n    integer k;\n    begin\n      m  = 11'd0;\n      sh = v;\n      for (k = 10; k >= 0; k = k - 1) begin\n        m  = m | ({11{oh[k]}} & sh);\n        sh = {sh[9:0], 1'b0};\n      end\n      ljust = m;\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  // 3c. binary encode of the leading-one index from the one-hot\n  function [3:0] enc11;\n    input [10:0] oh;\n    begin\n      enc11 = {|oh[10:8], |oh[7:4], (|oh[7:6]) | (|oh[3:2]),\n               oh[9] | oh[7] | oh[5] | oh[3] | oh[1]};\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  wire [10:0] v_a = {~e_a_zero, f_a};\n  wire [10:0] v_b = {~e_b_zero, f_b};\n  wire [10:0] oh_a = lead1oh(v_a);\n  wire [10:0] oh_b = lead1oh(v_b);\n  wire [10:0] m_a  = ljust(v_a, oh_a);\n  wire [10:0] m_b  = ljust(v_b, oh_b);\n\n  // shifted biased exponents: EA = e' + 9  (e' = i-9 for subnormals, e for normals)\n  wire [5:0] EA = e_a_zero ? {2'b00, enc11(oh_a)} : (6'd9 + e_a);\n  wire [5:0] EB = e_b_zero ? {2'b00, enc11(oh_b)} : (6'd9 + e_b);\n\n  // 4. significand product and one-step normalization (leading one onto bit 21)\n  wire [21:0] prod = m_a * m_b;\n  wire        adj  = prod[21];\n  wire [10:0] gP11  = adj ? prod[21:11] : prod[20:10];   // P[21:11]\n  wire        gGd   = adj ? prod[10]    : prod[9];       // P[10]  (guard)\n  wire        stLo  = adj ? |prod[9:0]  : |prod[8:0];    // |P[9:0]\n\n  // 5. normal path (U2 >= 34): frac = gP11[9:0], guard = gGd, lsb = gP11[0]\n  wire        n_round = gGd & (stLo | gP11[0]);\n  wire [9:0]  frac_n  = gP11[9:0];\n  wire [9:0]  fA      = andpre10(frac_n);\n  wire [9:0]  frac_r;\n  genvar g;\n  generate\n    assign frac_r[0] = frac_n[0] ^ n_round;\n    for (g = 1; g <= 9; g = g + 1)\n      assign frac_r[g] = frac_n[g] ^ (n_round & fA[g-1]);\n  endgenerate\n  wire c = n_round & fA[9];   // rounding carry into the exponent\n\n  // 6. exponent: U2 = EA + EB + adj; biased result exponent = U2 - 33 (+ carry)\n  wire [6:0] U2      = {1'b0, EA} + {1'b0, EB} + adj;\n  wire       sub_is  = (U2 <= 7'd33);\n  wire [6:0] exp_pre = U2 - 7'd33;                  // 1..48 on the normal path\n  wire [6:0] exp_hi  = exp_pre + 7'd1;\n  wire [5:0] n_exp   = c ? exp_hi[5:0] : exp_pre[5:0];\n  wire       n_inf   = exp_pre[5] | (&exp_pre[4:0])\n                      | (c & (exp_pre[4:0] == 5'd30) & ~exp_pre[5]);\n  wire [4:0] exp_n   = n_inf ? 5'd31 : n_exp[4:0];\n\n  // 7. subnormal path (U2 <= 33); clamped qv = 11 yields the rounded zero\n  wire        qclamp = (U2 <= 7'd22);\n  wire [5:0]  qv0    = 6'd33 - U2[5:0];\n  wire [3:0]  qv     = qclamp ? 4'd11 : qv0[3:0];\n  wire [10:0] w1 = qv[3] ? {8'b00000000, gP11[10:8]} : gP11;\n  wire        q1 = qv[3] & |gP11[7:0];\n  wire [10:0] w2 = qv[2] ? {4'b0000, w1[10:4]} : w1;\n  wire        q2 = q1 | (qv[2] & |w1[3:0]);\n  wire [10:0] w3 = qv[1] ? {2'b00, w2[10:2]} : w2;\n  wire        q3 = q2 | (qv[1] & |w2[1:0]);\n  wire [10:0] W  = qv[0] ? {1'b0, w3[10:1]} : w3;\n  wire        q4 = q3 | (qv[0] & w3[0]);\n  wire        stLo2 = |prod[9:0] | (adj & prod[10]);   // |P[10:0]\n  wire        s_sticky = q4 | stLo2;\n  wire        s_round  = W[0] & (s_sticky | W[1]);\n  wire [9:0]  frac_s0  = W[10:1];\n  wire [9:0]  sA       = andpre10(frac_s0);\n  wire [9:0]  frac_sr;\n  generate\n    assign frac_sr[0] = frac_s0[0] ^ s_round;\n    for (g = 1; g <= 9; g = g + 1)\n      assign frac_sr[g] = frac_s0[g] ^ (s_round & sA[g-1]);\n  endgenerate\n  wire csub = s_round & sA[9];   // rounded up to the smallest normal\n\n  // 8. select / pack\n  wire [4:0] exp_g  = sub_is ? {4'd0, csub} : exp_n;\n  wire [9:0] frac_g = sub_is ? frac_sr : frac_r;\n\n  wire res_inf  = ~sub_is & n_inf;\n  wire is_nan   = nan_in;\n  wire is_inf   = ~is_nan & (inf_in | res_inf);\n  wire is_zero  = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & zero_in;\n\n  wire [4:0] exp_y  = (is_nan | is_inf) ? 5'd31 : is_zero ? 5'd0 : exp_g;\n  wire [9:0] frac_y = is_nan ? 10'h200 : (is_inf | is_zero) ? 10'd0 : frac_g;\n\n  assign y = is_nan ? 16'h7E00 : {s_y, exp_y, frac_y};\n\n  // parallel-prefix AND (c[j] = &x[j:0], depth 4)\n  function [9:0] andpre10;\n    input [9:0] x;\n    integer j, l;\n    reg [9:0] c;\n    begin\n      c = x;\n      for (l = 0; l < 4; l = l + 1)\n        for (j = 9; j >= (1 << l); j = j - 1)\n          c[j] = c[j] & c[j - (1 << l)];\n      andpre10 = c;\n    end\n  endfunction\nendmodule\n", filename='design_v10.v')

[ASSISTANT]
v10 passed but was slightly worse (137819 vs v8's 135571). The critical path is the subnormal path: EA/EB → U2 (includes the late `adj`) → shift amount → 4 shift stages → rounding → muxes. Key insight: the subnormal extraction can be made **adj-free** — anchoring the window at `prod[10]` so `W[j] = prod[10+j+qn]` with `qn = 33-(EA+EB)` computed early, plus a truncated multiplier (10×10 + small add, low bits only OR-reduced for sticky). Building v11:
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- IEEE binary16 multiplier (1 sign, 5 exponent, 10 fraction bits) with subnormal support.\n// v11: per-operand normalization + truncated multiplier + adj-free subnormal shift.\n// Each nonzero operand significand ma is normalized to have its leading one at bit 10\n// (normals: {1,f}; subnormals: fraction left-justified with the exponent decremented),\n// so ma = 2^10 + x and the product prod = 2^20 + 2^10*(x+y) + x*y.\n// Only prod[21:10] (prod_hi) and |prod[9:0] (sticky_low) are computed in full:\n//   prod_hi = 2^10 + (x+y) + (x*y)[19:10],  sticky_low = |(x*y)[9:0]|.\n// Unified exponent U2 = EA + EB + adj (biased result exponent U2-33) drives:\n//   - normal results (U2 >= 34): frac = prod[20+adj-... ] i.e. frac[j] = prod[10+j+adj],\n//     guard = prod[9+adj], standard RNE rounding.\n//   - subnormal results (U2 <= 33): M = prod >> (11+qn), qn = 33-(EA+EB)  [adj-free!],\n//     guard = prod[10+qn], frac[j] = end\n      ljust = m;\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  // 3c. binary encode of the leading-one index from the one-hot\n  function [3:0] enc11;\n    input [10:0] oh;\n    begin\n      enc11 = {|oh[10:8], wire [10:0] oh_a = lead1oh(v_a);\n  wire [10:0] oh_b = lead1oh(v_b);\n  wire [10:0] m_a  = ljust(v_a, oh_a);\n  wire [10:0] m_b  = ljust(v_b, oh_b);\n\n  // shifted biased exponents: EA = e' + 9  (e'
