[ASSISTANT]
Found it by careful re-derivation: in v13 the subnormal shift-amount clamp is wrong. `qn = 33-(EA+EB)` can reach 19, but my `qbig` condition misses values 16–19 (e.g. `qn=19` → `qn_c=3`), so the frac window catches product bits that should be shifted out. The clamp must be `qn7 >= 12`: `qn7[6]|qn7[5]|qn7[4]|(qn7[3]&qn7[2])`. Everything else I re-verified (P = prod·2^(1-adj), E' = U2−33, k = prod·2^(EA+EB−44), guard = prod[10+qn], frac[j] = prod[11+j+qn]). Creating v14:
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- IEEE binary16 multiplier (1 sign, 5 exponent, 10 fraction bits) with subnormal support.\n// v14: per-operand normalization + truncated multiplier + adj-free subnormal shift.\n// Each nonzero operand significand m is normalized to have its leading one at bit 10\n// (normals: {1,f}; subnormals: fraction left-justified with the exponent decremented),\n// so m = 2^10 + t and the product prod = 2^20 + 2^10*(ta+tb) + ta*tb.\n// Only prod[21:10] (prod_hi) and |prod[9:0] (sticky_low) are computed in full:\n//   prod_hi = 2^10 + (ta+tb) + (ta*tb)[19:10],  sticky_low = |(ta*tb)[9:0]|.\n// With P = prod<<(1-adj) (adj = prod[21], so P's leading one is at bit 21) and\n// E' = U2-33 (U2 = EA+EB+adj):  value = P * 2^(E'-36).\n//   - normal results (E' >= 1): frac = P[20:11], guard = P[10], sticky = |P[9:0].\n//   - subnormal results (E' <= 0): k = value*2^24 = prod*2^(EA+EB-44)  [adj-free!],\n//     so with qn = 33-(EA+EB): guard = prod[10+qn], frac[j] = prod[11+j+qn],\n//     sticky = |prod[9+qn:0] (accumulated through the shift stages + sticky_low).\n//     qn >= 12 => guard = 0 and frac = 0 => rounded zero automatically &e_a;\n  wire e_b_ones =  &e_b;\n  wire f_a_zero = ~|f_a;\n  wire f_b_zero = ~|f_b;\n  wire zero_a   = e_a_zero &  f_a_zero;\n  wire zero_b   = e_b_zero &  f_b_zero;\n  wire nan_a    = e_a_ones & ~f_a_zero;\n  wire nan_b    = e_b_ones & ~f_b_zero;\n  wire inf_a    = e_a_ones &  f_a_zero;\n  wire inf_b    = e_b_ones &  f_b_zero;\n\n  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  // 3a. one-hot of the leading one of an 11-bit vector (all zero if v == 0)\n  function [10:0] lead1oh;\n    input [10:0] v;\n    reg [10:0] g, oh;\n    integer l, k;\n    begin\n      g = v;\n      for (l = 1; l <= 4; l = l + 1)\n        for (k = 10; k >= 1; k = k - 1)\n          if (k + (1 << (l-1)) <= 10)\n            g[k] = g[k] | g[k + (1 << (l-1))];   // g[k] = |v[10:k]\n      oh[10] = v[10];\n      for (k = 9; k >= 0; k = k - 1)\n        oh[k] = v[k] & ~g[k+1];\n      lead1oh = oh;\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  // 3b. left-justify v's leading one onto bit 10 (identity if it is already there)\n  function [10:0] ljust;\n    input [10:0] v;\n    input [10:0] oh;\n    reg [10:0] m, sh;\n    integer k;\n    begin\n      m  = 11'd0;\n      sh = v;\n      for (k = 10; k >= 0; k = k - 1) begin\n        m  = m | ({11{oh[k]}} & sh);\n        sh = {sh[9:0], 1'b0};\n      end\n      ljust = m;\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  // 3c. binary encode of the leading-one index from the one-hot\n  function [3:0] enc11;\n    input [10:0] oh;\n    begin\n      enc11 = {|oh[10:8], |oh[7:4], (|oh[7:6]) | (|oh[3:2]),\n               oh[9] | oh[7] | oh[5] | oh[3] | oh[1]};\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  wire [10:0] v_a = {~e_a_zero, f_a};\n  wire [10:0] v_b = {~e_b_zero, f_b};\n subnormals: fraction left-justified with the exponent decremented),\n// so m = 2^10 + t and the product prod = 2^20 + 2^10*(ta+tb) + ta*tb.\n// Only prod[21:10] (prod_hi) and |prod[9:0] (sticky_low) are computed in full:\n//   prod_hi = {1'b1, TT[19:10]} + S1  (S1 = ta+tb),  adj = prod_hi[11] (carry).\n// Result exponent E' = EA+EB+adj-33, computed as E0 = EA+EB-33 (early) plus the late\n// 1-bit increment inc = adj | round_carry:\n//   - normal results (E' >= 1): frac = prod[19+adj:10+adj], guard = prod[9+adj],\n//     sticky = |prod[8+adj:0], standard RNE rounding.\n//   - subnormal results (E' <= 0): qn = 33-(EA+EB) (early, adj-free),\n//     guard = prod[10+qn], frac[j] = prod[11+j+qn], sticky = discarded bits + sticky_low.\n//     qn >= 12 => guard = frac = 0 => rounded zero automatically (clamped shift).\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  // 1. unpack\n  wire        s_a = a[15];\n  wire        s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0]  e_a = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0]  e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0]  f_a = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0]  f_b = b[9:0];\n\n  // 2. classify\n  wire e_a_zero = ~|e_a;\n  wire e_b_zero = ~|e_b;\n  wire e_a_ones =  &e_a;\n  wire e_b_ones =  &e_b;\n  wire f_a_zero = ~|f_a;\n  wire f_b_zero = ~|f_b;\n  wire zero_a   = e_a_zero &  f_a_zero;\n  wire zero_b   = e_b_zero &  f_b_zero;\n  wire nan_a    = e_a_ones & ~f_a_zero;\n  wire nan_b    = e_b_ones & ~f_b_zero;\n  wire inf_a    = e_a_ones &  f_a_zero;\n  wire inf_b    = e_b_ones &  f_b_zero;\n\n  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  // 3a. one-hot of the leading one of an 11-bit vector (all zero if v == 0)\n  function [10:0] lead1oh;\n    input [10:0] v;\n    reg [10:0] g, oh;\n    integer l, k;\n    begin\n      g = v;\n      for (l = 1; l <= 4; l = l + 1)\n        for (k = 10; k >= 1; k = k - 1)\n          if (k + (1 << (l-1)) <= 10)\n            g[k] = g[k] | g[k + (1 << (l-1))];   // g[k] = |v[10:k]\n      oh[10] = v[10];\n      for (k = 9; k >= 0; k = k - 1)\n        oh[k] = v[k] & ~g[k+1];\n      lead1oh = oh;\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  // 3b. left-justify v's leading one onto bit 10 (identity if it is already there)\n  function [10:0] ljust;\n    input [10:0] v;\n    input [10:0] oh;\n    reg [10:0] m, sh;\n    integer k;\n    begin\n      m  = 11'd0;\n      sh = v;\n      for (k = 10; k >= 0; k = k - 1) begin\n        m  = m | ({11{oh[k]}} & sh);\n        sh = {sh[9:0], 1'b0};\n      end\n      ljust = m;\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  // 3c. binary encode of the leading-one index from the one-hot\n  function [3:0] enc11;\n    input [10:0] oh;\n    begin\n      enc11 = {|oh[10:8], |oh[7:4], (|oh[7:6]) | (|oh[3:2]),\n               oh[9] | oh[7] | oh[5] | oh[3] | oh[1]};\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  wire [10:0] v_a = {~e_a_zero, f_a};\n  wire [10:0] v_b = {~e_b_zero, f_b};\n  wire [10:0] oh_a = lead1oh(v_a);\n  wire [10:0] oh_b = lead1oh(v_b);\n  wire [10:0] m_a  = ljust(v_a, oh_a);\n  wire [10:0] m_b  = ljust(v_b, oh_b);\n\n  // shifted biased exponents: EA = e' + 9  (e' = i-9 for subnormals, e for normals)\n  wire [5:0] EA = e_a_zero ? {2'b00, enc11(oh_a)} : (6'd9 + e_a);\n  wire [5:0] EB = e_b_zero ? {2'b00, enc11(oh_b)} : (6'd9 + e_b);\n\n  // 4. product: prod = 2^20 + 2^10*(ta+tb) + ta*tb; prod_hi = prod[21:10]\n  wire [9:0]  ta = m_a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0]  tb = m_b[9:0];\n  wire [19:0] TT = ta * tb;\n  wire [10:0] S1 = {1'b0, ta} + {1'b0, tb};\n  wire [11:0] prod_hi = {1'b1, TT[19:10]} + {1'b0, S1};\n  wire        adj        = prod_hi[11];\n  wire        sticky_low = |TT[9:0];\n  wire        sticky_lw0 = |TT[8:0];\n\n  // 5. early (adj-free) exponent bookkeeping\n  wire [6:0] U2n   = {1'b0, EA} + {1'b0, EB};\n  wire [6:0] E0    = U2n - 7'd33;                       // E' without adj/inc\n  wire [6:0] qn7   = 7'd33 - {1'b0, EA} - {1'b0, EB};   // subnormal shift\n  wire       qbig  = qn7[6] | qn7[5] | qn7[4] | (qn7[3] & qn7[2]);\n  wire [3:0] qn_c  = qbig ? 4'd15 : qn7[3:0];\n  wire       sub33 = (U2n <= 7'd33);\n  wire       eq33  = (U2n == 7'd33);\n\n  // 6. normal path: frac = prod[19+adj:10+adj], guard = prod[9+adj], sticky below\n  wire [9:0] frac_n  = adj ? prod_hi[10:1] : prod_hi[9:0];\n  wire       guard_n = adj ? prod_hi[0]   : TT[9];\n  wire       stLo    = adj ? sticky_low   : sticky_lw0;\n  wire       n_round = guard_n & (stLo | frac_n[0]);\n  wire [9:0] fA      = andpre10(frac_n);\n  wire [9:0] frac_r;\n  genvar g;\n  generate\n    assign frac_r[0] = frac_n[0] ^ n_round;\n    for (g = 1; g <= 9; g = g + 1)\n      assign frac_r[g] = frac_n[g] ^ (n_round & fA[g-1]);\n  endgenerate\n  wire c = n_round & fA[9];   // rounding carry into the exponent\n\n  // final exponent: E0 + inc, inc = adj | c\n  wire       inc    = adj | c;\n  wire [6:0] exp_v  = E0 + {6'd0, inc};\n  wire       e_ge31 = E0[6] | E0[5] | (&E0[4:0]);\n  wire       e_eq30 = (E0 == 7'd30);\n  wire       n_inf  = e_ge31 | (e_eq30 & inc);\n  wire [4:0] exp_n  = n_inf ? 5'd31 : exp_v[4:0];\n\n  // 7. subnormal path: guard = prod[10+qn], frac[j] = prod[11+j+qn] via a 12-bit\n  //    window V = prod[21:10] >> qn; sticky = discarded bits + sticky_low\n  wire [15:0] V0 = {4'b0000, prod_hi};\n  wire [15:0] V1 = qn_c[0] ? {1'b0, V0[15:1]} : V0;\n  wire        q1 = qn_c[0] & V0[0];\n  wire [15:0] V2 = qn_c[1] ? {2'b00, V1[15:2]} : V1;\n  wire        q2 = q1 | (qn_c[1] & (|V1[1:0]));\n  wire [15:0] V3 = qn_c[2] ? {4'b0000, V2[15:4]} : V2;\n  wire        q3 = q2 | (qn_c[2] & (|V2[3:0]));\n  wire [15:0] V4 = qn_c[3] ? {8'b00000000, V3[15:8]} : V3;\n  wire        q4 = q3 | (qn_c[3] & (|V3[7:0]));\n  wire [10:0] W  = V4[10:0];\n  wire        s_sticky = q4 | sticky_low;\n  wire        s_round  = W[0] & (s_sticky | W[1]);\n  wire [9:0]  frac_s0  = W[10:1];\n  wire [9:0]  sA       = andpre10(frac_s0);\n  wire [9:0]  frac_sr;\n  generate\n    assign frac_sr[0] = frac_s0[0] ^ s_round;\n    for (g = 1; g <= 9; g = g + 1)\n      assign frac_sr[g] = frac_s0[g] ^ (s_round & sA[g-1]);\n  endgenerate\n  wire csub = s_round & sA[9];   // rounded up to the smallest normal\n\n  // 8. select / pack\n  wire       sub_is = sub33 | (adj & eq33);\n  wire [4:0] exp_g  = sub_is ? {4'd0, csub} : exp_n;\n  wire [9:0] frac_g = sub_is ? frac_sr : frac_r;\n\n  wire res_inf  = ~sub_is & n_inf;\n  wire is_nan   = nan_in;\n  wire is_inf   = ~is_nan & (inf_in | res_inf);\n  wire is_zero  = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & zero_in;\n\n  wire [4:0] exp_y  = (is_nan | is_inf) ? 5'd31 : is_zero ? 5'd0 : exp_g;\n  wire [9:0] frac_y = is_nan ? 10'h200 : (is_inf | is_zero) ? 10'd0 : frac_g;\n\n  assign y = is_nan ? 16'h7E00 : {s_y, exp_y, frac_y};\n\n  // parallel-prefix AND (c[j] = &x[j:0], depth 4)\n  function [9:0] andpre10;\n    input [9:0] x;\n    integer j, l;\n    reg [9:0] c;\n    begin\n      c = x;\n      for (l = 0; l < 4; l = l + 1)\n        for (j = 9; j >= (1 << l); j = j - 1)\n          c[j] = c[j] & c[j - (1 << l)];\n      andpre10 = c;\n    end\n  endfunction\nendmodule\n", filename='design_v15.v')

  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- IEEE binary16 multiplier (1 sign, 5 exponent, 10 fraction bits) with subnormal support.\n// v15b: per-operand normalization + truncated multiplier + adj-free subnormal shift +\n// early (adj-free) exponent bookkeeping with a late 2-select increment mux.\n// Each nonzero operand significand m is normalized to have its leading one at bit 10\n// (normals: {1,f}; subnormals: fraction left-justified with the exponent decremented),\n// so m = 2^10 + t and prod = m_a*m_b = 2^20 + 2^10*(ta+tb) + ta*tb, with\n//   prod_hi = prod[21:10] = 2^10 + S1 + TT[19:10]   (S1 = ta+tb, TT = ta*tb),\n//   adj = prod_hi[11] (carry: leading one of prod at bit 21 vs 20).\n// P = prod << (1-adj) has its leading one at bit 21; result exponent E' = EA+EB+adj-33\n// (+1 if rounding carries).  With S = EA+EB (early): E' = E0 + adj + c, E0 = S-33.\n//   - normal results (E' >= 1): frac[j] = prod[10+j+adj], guard = prod[9+adj],\n//     sticky = |prod[8+adj:0]; X = E'+c <= 29 so exp = X[4:0] and inf <=> X == 31.\n//   - subnormal results (E' <= 0): qn = 33-S (early, adj-free): guard = prod[10+qn],\n//     frac[j] = prod[11+j+qn], sticky = discarded bits + |prod[9:0].\n//     qn >= 12 => guard = frac = 0 => rounded zero automatically (clamped shift).\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  // 1. unpack\n  wire        s_a = a[15];\n  wire        s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0]  e_a = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0]  e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0]  f_a = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0]  f_b = b[9:0];\n\n  // 2. classify\n  wire e_a_zero = ~|e_a;\n  wire e_b_zero = ~|e_b;\n  wire e_a_ones =  &e_a;\n  wire e_b_ones =  &e_b;\n  wire f_a_zero = ~|f_a;\n  wire f_b_zero = ~|f_b;\n  wire zero_a   = e_a_zero &  f_a_zero;\n  wire zero_b   = e_b_zero &  f_b_zero;\n  wire nan_a    = e_a_ones & ~f_a_zero;\n  wire nan_b    = e_b_ones & ~f_b_zero;\n  wire inf_a    = e_a_ones &  f_a_zero;\n  wire inf_b    = e_b_ones &  f_b_zero;\n\n  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  // 3a. one-hot of the leading one of an 11-bit vector (all zero if v == 0)\n  function [10:0] lead1oh;\n    input [10:0] v;\n    reg [10:0] g, oh;\n    integer l, k;\n    begin\n      g = v;\n      for (l = 1; l <= 4; l = l + 1)\n        for (k = 10; k >= 1; k = k - 1)\n          if (k + (1 << (l-1)) <= 10)\n            g[k] = g[k] | g[k + (1 << (l-1))];   // g[k] = |v[10:k]\n      oh[10] = v[10];\n      for (k = 9; k >= 0; k = k - 1)\n        oh[k] = v[k] & ~g[k+1];\n      lead1oh = oh;\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  // 3b. left-justify v's leading one onto bit 10 (identity if it is already there)\n  function [10:0] ljust;\n    input [10:0] v;\n    input [10:0] oh;\n    reg [10:0] m, sh;\n    integer k;\n    begin\n      m  = 11'd0;\n      sh = v;\n      for (k = 10; k >= 0; k = k - 1) begin\n        m  = m | ({11{oh[k]}} & sh);\n        sh = {sh[9:0], 1'b0};\n      end\n      ljust = m;\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  // 3c. binary encode of the leading-one index from the one-hot\n  function [3:0] enc11;\n    input [10:0] oh;\n    begin\n      enc11 = {|oh[10:8], |oh[7:4], (|oh[7:6]) | (|oh[3:2]),\n               oh[9] | oh[7] | oh[5] | oh[3] | oh[1]};\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  wire [10:0] v_a = {~e_a_zero, f_a};\n  wire [10:0] v_b = {~e_b_zero, f_b};\n  wire [10:0] oh_a = lead1oh(v_a);\n  wire [10:0] oh_b = lead1oh(v_b);\n  wire [10:0] m_a  = ljust(v_a, oh_a);\n  wire [10:0] m_b  = ljust(v_b, oh_b);\n\n  // shifted biased exponents: EA = e' + 9  (e' = i-9 for subnormals, e for normals)\n  wire [5:0] EA = e_a_zero ? {2'b00, enc11(oh_a)} : (6'd9 + e_a);\n  wire [5:0] EB = e_b_zero ? {2'b00, enc11(oh_b)} : (6'd9 + e_b);\n\n  // 4. product: prod = 2^20 + 2^10*S1 + TT ; Reproduced — so it's a real bug. I found it: for `S = EA+EB` up to 78, `S[6]` can be 1 while `S[5]`=0 (78 = 1001110), so my `le32/le33` subnormal tests (`~S[5] | ...`) wrongly fire for large exponents → overflow cases output zero. They must exclude `S[6]`:
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- IEEE binary16 multiplier (1 sign, 5 exponent, 10 fraction bits) with subnormal support.\n// v15c: per-operand normalization + truncated multiplier + adj-free subnormal shift +\n// early (adj-free) exponent bookkeeping with a late 2-select increment mux.\n// Each nonzero operand significand m is normalized to have its leading one at bit 10\n// (normals: {1,f}; subnormals: fraction left-justified with the exponent decremented),\n// so m = 2^10 + t and prod = m_a*m_b = 2^20 + 2^10*(ta+tb) + ta*tb, with\n//   prod_hi = prod[21:10] = 2^10 + S1 + TT[19:10]   (S1 = ta+tb, TT = ta*tb),\n//   adj = prod_hi[11] (carry: leading one of prod at bit 21 vs 20).\n// P = prod << (1-adj) has its leading one at bit 21; result exponent E' = EA+EB+adj-33\n// (+1 if rounding carries).  With S = EA+EB (early): E' = E0 + adj + c, E0 = S-33.\n//   - normal results (E' >= 1): frac[j] = prod[10+j+adj], guard = prod[9+adj],\n//     sticky = |prod[8+adj:0]; X = E'+c <= 29 so exp = X[4:0] and inf <=> X == 31.\n//   - subnormal results (E' <= 0): qn = 33-S (early, adj-free): guard = prod[10+qn],\n//     frac[j] = prod[11+j+qn], sticky = discarded bits + |prod[9:0].\n//     qn >= 12 => guard = frac = 0 => rounded zero automatically (clamped shift).\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  // 1. unpack\n  wire        s_a = a[15];\n  wire        s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0]  e_a = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0]  e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0]  f_a = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0]  f_b = b[9:0];\n\n  // 2. classify\n  wire e_a_zero = ~|e_a;\n  wire e_b_zero = ~|e_b;\n  wire e_a_ones =  &e_a;\n  wire e_b_ones =  &e_b;\n  wire f_a_zero = ~|f_a;\n  wire f_b_zero = ~|f_b;\n  wire zero_a   = e_a_zero &  f_a_zero;\n  wire zero_b   = e_b_zero &  f_b_zero;\n  wire nan_a    = e_a_ones & ~f_a_zero;\n  wire nan_b    = e_b_ones & ~f_b_zero;\n  wire inf_a    = e_a_ones &  f_a_zero;\n  wire inf_b    = e_b_ones &  f_b_zero;\n\n  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  // 3a. one-hot of the leading one of an 11-bit vector (all zero if v == 0)\n  function [10:0] lead1oh;\n    input [10:0] v;\n    reg [10:0] g, oh;\n    integer l, k;\n    begin\n      g = v;\n      for (l = 1; l <= 4; l = l + 1)\n        for (k = 10; k >= 1; k = k - 1)\n          if (k + (1 << (l-1)) <= 10)\n            g[k] = g[k] | g[k + (1 << (l-1))];   // g[k] = |v[10:k]\n      oh[10] = v[10];\n      for (k = 9; k >= 0; k = k - 1)\n        oh[k] = v[k] & ~g[k+1];\n      lead1oh = oh;\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  // 3b. left-justify v's leading one onto bit 10 (identity if it is already there)\n  function [10:0] ljust;\n    input [10:0] v;\n    input [10:0] oh;\n    reg [10:0] m, sh;\n    integer k;\n    begin\n      m  = 11'd0;\n      sh = v;\n      for (k = 10; k >= 0; k = k - 1) begin\n        m  = m | ({11{oh[k]}} & sh);\n        sh = {sh[9:0], 1'b0};\n      end\n      ljust = m;\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  // 3c. binary encode of the leading-one index from the one-hot\n  function [3:0] enc11;\n    input [10:0] oh;\n    begin\n      enc11 = {|oh[10:8], |oh[7:4], (|oh[7:6]) | (|oh[3:2]),\n               oh[9] | oh[7] | oh[5] | oh[3] | oh[1]};\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  wire [10:0] v_a = {~e_a_zero, f_a};\n  wire [10:0] v_b = {~e_b_zero, f_b};\n  wire [10:0] oh_a = lead1oh(v_a);\n  wire [10:0] oh_b = lead1oh(v_b);\n  wire [10:0] m_a  = ljust(v_a, oh_a);\n  wire [10:0] m_b  = ljust(v_b, oh_b);\n\n  // shifted biased exponents: EA = e' + 9  (e' = i-9 for subnormals, e for normals)\n  wire [5:0] EA = e_a_zero ? {2'b00, enc11(oh_a)} : (6'd9 + e_a);\n  wire [5:0] EB = e_b_zero ? {2'b00, enc11(oh_b)} : (6'd9 + e_b);\n\n  // 4. product: prod = 2^20 + 2^10*S1 + TT ; prod_hi = prod[21:10]\n  wire [9:0]  ta = m_a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0]  tb = m_b[9:0];\n  wire [19:0] TT = ta * tb;\n  wire [10:0] S1 = {1'b0, ta} + {1'b0, tb};\n  wire [11:0] prod_hi = 12'd1024 + {1'b0, S1} + {2'b0, TT[19:10]};\n  wire        adj        = prod_hi[11];\n  wire        sticky_low = |TT[9:0];\n  wire        sticky_lw0 = |TT[8:0];\n\n  // 5. early (adj-free) exponent bookkeeping: S = EA+EB (18..126), E0 = S-33 (-15..93)\n  wire [6:0] S    = {1'b0, EA} + {1'b0, EB};\n  wire [6:0] E0   = S - 7'd33;\n  wire [6:0] E0p1 = E0 + 7'd1;\n  wire [6:0] E0p2 = E0 + 7'd2;\n  // E0 >= 31 / 30 / 29 on the normal path (E0 <= 47 there, bit6 = 0):\n  wire       ge31 = E0[5] | (&E0[4:0]);\n  wire       ge30 = E0[5] | (E0[4] & E0[3] & E0[2] & E0[1]);\n  wire       ge29 = E0[5] | (E0[4] & E0[3] & E0[2] & (E0[1] | E0[0]));\n  // S <= 33+adj on the subnormal path:\n  wire       le33 = ~S[6] & (~S[5] | (~|S[4:1]));\n  wire       le32 = ~S[6] & (~S[5] | (~|S[4:0]));\n  wire [6:0] qn7  = 7'd33 - S;\n  wire       qbig = qn7[6] | qn7[5] | qn7[4] | (qn7[3] & qn7[2]);\n  wire [3:0] qn_c = qbig ? 4'd15 : qn7[3:0];\n\n  // 6. normal path: frac_n[j] = prod_hi[j+adj], guard = prod[9+adj], sticky below\n  wire [9:0] frac_n  = adj ? prod_hi[10:1] : prod_hi[9:0];\n  wire       guard_n = adj ? prod_hi[0]    : TT[9];\n  wire       stLo    = adj ? sticky_low    : sticky_lw0;\n  wire       n_round = guard_n & (stLo | frac_n[0]);\n  wire [9:0] fA      = andpre10(frac_n);\n  wire [9:0] frac_r;\n  genvar g;\n  generate\n    assign frac_r[0] = frac_n[0] ^ n_round;\n    for (g = 1; g <= 9; g = g + 1)\n      assign frac_r[g] = frac_n[g] ^ (n_round & fA[g-1]);\n  endgenerate\n  wire c = n_round & fA[9];   // rounding carry into the exponent\n\n  // 7. subnormal path: guard = prod[10+qn], frac[j] = prod[11+j+qn] via a 12-bit\n  //    window V = prod_hi >> qn; sticky = discarded bits + sticky_low\n  wire [15:0] V0 = {4'b0000, prod_hi};\n  wire [15:0] V1 = qn_c[0] ? {1'b0, V0[15:1]} : V0;\n  wire        q1 = qn_c[0] & V0[0];\n  wire [15:0] V2 = qn_c[1] ? {2'b00, V1[15:2]} : V1;\n  wire        q2 = q1 | (qn_c[1] & (|V1[1:0]));\n  wire [15:0] V3 = qn_c[2] ? {4'b0000, V2[15:4]} : V2;\n  wire        q3 = q2 | (qn_c[2] & (|V2[3:0]));\n  wire [15:0] V4 = qn_c[3] ? {8'b00000000, V3[15:8]} : V3;\n  wire        q4 = q3 | (qn_c[3] & (|V3[7:0]));\n  wire [10:0] W  = V4[10:0];\n  wire        s_sticky = q4 | sticky_low;\n  wire        s_round  = W[0] & (s_sticky | W[1]);\n  wire [9:0]  frac_s0  = W[10:1];\n  wire [9:0]  sA       = andpre10(frac_s0);\n  wire [9:0]  frac_sr;\n  generate\n    assign frac_sr[0] = frac_s0[0] ^ s_round;\n    for (g = 1; g <= 9; g = g + 1)\n      assign frac_sr[g] = frac_s0[g] ^ (s_round & sA[g-1]);\n  endgenerate\n  wire csub = s_round & sA[9];   // rounded up to the smallest normal\n\n  // 8. late exponent assembly: X = E0 + adj + c  (<= 29, so inf <=> X == 31)\n  wire       sel1   = adj | c;\n  wire       sel2   = adj & c;\n  wire [4:0] X5     = sel2 ? E0p2[4:0] : sel1 ? E0p1[4:0] : E0[4:0];\n  wire       n_inf  = sel2 ? ge29 : sel1 ? ge30 : ge31;\n  wire       sub_is = adj ? le32 : le33;\n\n  // 9. select / pack\n  wire [4:0] exp_g  = sub_is ? {4'd0, csub} : X5;\n  wire [9:0] frac_g = sub_is ? frac_sr : frac_r;\n\n  wire res_inf  = ~sub_is & n_inf;\n  wire is_nan   = nan_in;\n  wire is_inf   = ~is_nan & (inf_in | res_inf);\n  wire is_zero  = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & zero_in;\n\n  wire [4:0] exp_y  = is_zero ? 5'd0 : is_inf ? 5'd31 : exp_g;\n  wire [9:0] frac_y = is_nan ? 10'h200 : is_inf ? 10'd0 : is_zero ? 10'd0 : frac_g;\n\n  assign y = is_nan ? 16'h7E00 : {s_y, exp_y, frac_y};\n\n  // parallel-prefix AND (c[j] = &x[j:0], depth 4)\n  function [9:0] andpre10;\n    input [9:0] x;\n    integer j, l;\n    reg [9:0] c;\n    begin\n      c = x;\n      for (l = 0; l < 4; l = l + 1)\n        for (j = 9; j >= (1 << l); j = j - 1)\n          c[j] = c[j] & c[j - (1 << l)];\n      andpre10 = c;\n    end\n  endfunction\nendmodule\n", filename='design_v15c.v')

[ASSISTANT]
v15c **passes: 127340** @1300 → **121690**. The critical path is the front end + two cascaded adders (S1 = ta+tb, then prod_hi). v16: merge into a single 4-input CSA + explicit 12-bit Kogge-Stone prefix CPA, fast 2-level AND10 for the rounding carries, and fold the zero/inf overrides into the early exponent logic (removing late pack muxes). (I traced the prefix-adder carefully — sum must use the *original* bitwise XOR, not the group propagate.)
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- IEEE binary16 multiplier (1 sign, 5 exponent, 10 fraction bits) with subnormal support.\n// v16: per-operand normalization + truncated multiplier whose high slice is formed by a\n// single 4-input CSA followed by an explicit 12-bit Kogge-Stone prefix CPA (no cascaded\n// ripple adders), fast rounding-carry ANDs, and zero/inf handling folded into the early\n// exponent logic.\n// Each nonzero operand significand m is normalized to have its leading one at bit 10\n// (normals: {1,f}; subnormals: fraction left-justified with the exponent decremented),\n// so m = 2^10 + t and prod = m_a*m_b = 2^20 + 2^10*(ta+tb) + ta*tb, hence\n//   prod_hi = prod[21:10] = 1024 + ta + tb + TT[19:10]   (TT = ta*tb),\n//   adj = prod_hi[11], sticky_low = |TT[9:0]|.\n// value = prod * 2^(EA+EB-68); result exponent E' = EA+EB+adj-33 (+1 on rounding carry).\n//   - normal results (E' >= 1): frac[j] = prod[10+j+adj], guard = prod[9+adj].\n//   - subnormal results (E' <= 0): qn = 33-(EA+EB): guard = prod[10+qn],\n//     frac[j] = prod[11+j+qn], sticky = discarded bits + sticky_low; qn >= 12 => zero.\n// Zero operands force the (zero) subnormal path; input infinities fold into the early\n// overflow tests, so the pack stage needs no extra muxes.\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  // 1. unpack\n  wire        s_a = a[15];\n  wire        s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0]  e_a = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0]  e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0]  f_a = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0]  f_b = b[9:0];\n\n  // 2. classify\n  wire e_a_zero = ~|e_a;\n  wire e_b_zero = ~|e_b;\n  wire e_a_ones =  &e_a;\n  wire e_b_ones =  &e_b;\n  wire f_a_zero = ~|f_a;\n  wire f_b_zero = ~|f_b;\n  wire zero_a   = e_a_zero &  f_a_zero;\n  wire zero_b   = e_b_zero &  f_b_zero;\n  wire nan_a    = e_a_ones & ~f_a_zero;\n  wire nan_b    = e_b_ones & ~f_b_zero;\n  wire inf_a    = e_a_ones &  f_a_zero;\n  wire inf_b    = e_b_ones &  f_b_zero;\n\n  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  // 3a. one-hot of the leading one of an 11-bit vector (all zero if v == 0)\n  function [10:0] lead1oh;\n    input [10:0] v;\n    reg [10:0] g, oh;\n    integer l, k;\n    begin\n      g = v;\n      for (l = 1; l <= 4; l = l + 1)\n        for (k = 10; k >= 1; k = k - 1)\n          if (k + (1 << (l-1)) <= 10)\n            g[k] = g[k] | g[k + (1 << (l-1))];   // g[k] = |v[10:k]\n      oh[10] = v[10];\n      for (k = 9; k >= 0; k = k - 1)\n        oh[k] = v[k] & ~g[k+1];\n      lead1oh = oh;\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  // 3b. left-justify v's leading one onto bit 10 (identity if it is already there)\n  function [10:0] ljust;\n    input [10:0] v;\n    input [10:0] oh;\n    reg [10:0] m, sh;\n    integer k;\n    begin\n      m  = 11'd0;\n      sh = v;\n      for (k = 10; k >= 0; k = k - 1) begin\n        m  = m | ({11{oh[k]}} & sh);\n        sh = {sh[9:0], 1'b0};\n      end\n      ljust = m;\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  // 3c. binary encode of the leading-one index from the one-hot\n  function [3:0] enc11;\n    input [10:0] oh;\n    begin\n      enc11 = {|oh[10:8], |oh[7:4], (|oh[7:6]) | (|oh[3:2]),\n               oh[9] | oh[7] | oh[5] | oh[3] | oh[1]};\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  wire [10:0] v_a = {~e_a_zero, f_a};\n  wire [10:0] v_b = {~e_b_zero, f_b};\n  wire [10:0] oh_a = lead1oh(v_a);\n  wire [10:0] oh_b = lead1oh(v_b);\n  wire [10:0] m_a  = ljust(v_a, oh_a);\n  wire [10:0] m_b  = ljust(v_b, oh_b);\n\n  // shifted biased exponents: EA = e' + 9  (e' = i-9 for subnormals, e for normals)\n  wire [5:0] EA = e_a_zero ? {2'b00, enc11(oh_a)} : (6'd9 + e_a);\n  wire [5:0] EB = e_b_zero ? {2'b00, enc11(oh_b)} : (6'd9 + e_b);\n\n  // 4. product slices: TT = ta*tb; prod_hi = 1024 + ta + tb + TT[19:10]\n  wire [9:0]  ta = m_a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0]  tb = m_b[9:0];\n  wire [19:0] TT = ta * tb;\n\n  wire [11:0] A12 = {2'b00, ta};\n  wire [11:0] B12 = {2'b00, tb};\n  wire [11:0] C12 = {2'b00, TT[19:10]};\n  wire [11:0] D12 = 12'd1024;\n  wire [11:0] m1  = (A12 & B12) | (A12 & C12) | (B12 & C12);\n  wire [11:0] C1v = {m1[10:0], 1'b0};\n  wire [11:0] S1v = A12 ^ B12 ^ C12;\n  wire [11:0] S2  = S1v ^ C1v ^ D12;\n  wire [11:0] m2  = (S1v & C1v) | (S1v & D12) | (C1v & D12);\n  wire [11:0] C2v = {m2[10:0], 1'b0};\n  wire [11:0] prod_hi = pfx_add12(S2, C2v);\n\n  wire        adj        = prod_hi[11];\n  wire        sticky_low = |TT[9:0];\n  wire        sticky_lw0 = |TT[8:0];\n\n  // 5. early (adj-free) exponent bookkeeping: S = EA+EB (18..126), E0 = S-33\n  wire [6:0] S    = {1'b0, EA} + {1'b0, EB};\n  wire [6:0] E0   = S - 7'd33;\n  wire [6:0] E0p1 = E0 + 7'd1;\n  wire [6:0] E0p2 = E0 + 7'd2;\n  wire       ge31 = E0[5] | (&E0[4:0]);\n  wire       ge30 = E0[5] | (E0[4] & E0[3] & E0[2] & E0[1]);\n  wire       ge29 = E0[5] | (E0[4] & E0[3] & E0[2] & (E0[1] | E0[0]));\n  wire       le33 = ~S[6] & (~S[5] | (~|S[4:1]));\n  wire       le32 = ~S[6] & (~S[5] | (~|S[4:0]));\n  wire [6:0] qn7  = 7'd33 - S;\n  wire       qbig = qn7[6] | qn7[5] | qn7[4] | (qn7[3] & qn7[2]) | zero_in;\n  wire [3:0] qn_c = qbig ? 4'd15 : qn7[3:0];\n\n  // 6. normal path: frac_n[j] = prod_hi[j+adj], guard = prod[9+adj], sticky below\n  wire [9:0] frac_n  = adj ? prod_hi[10:1] : prod_hi[9:0];\n  wire       guard_n = adj ? prod_hi[0]    : TT[9];\n  wire       stLo    = adj ? sticky_low    : sticky_lw0;\n  wire       n_round = guard_n & (stLo | frac_n[0]);\n  wire       c       = n_round & &frac_n[9:5] & &frac_n[4:0];\n  wire [9:0] fA      = andpre10(frac_n);\n  wire [9:0] frac_r;\n  genvar g;\n  generate\n    assign frac_r[0] = frac_n[0] ^ n_round;\n    for (g = 1; g <= 8; g = g + 1)\n      assign frac_r[g] = frac_n[g] ^ (n_round & fA[g-1]);\n  endgenerate\n  assign frac_r[9] = frac_n[9] ^ c;\n\n  // 7. subnormal path: guard = prod[10+qn], frac[j] = prod[11+j+qn] via a 12-bit\n  //    window V = prod_hi >> qn; sticky = discarded bits + sticky_low\n  wire [15:0] V0 = {4'b0000, prod_hi};\n  wire [15:0] V1 = qn_c[0] ? {1'b0, V0[15:1]} : V0;\n  wire        q1 = qn_c[0] & V0[0];\n  wire [15:0] V2 = qn_c[1] ? {2'b00, V1[15:2]} : V1;\n  wire        q2 = q1 | (qn_c[1] & (|V1[1:0]));\n  wire [15:0] V3 = qn_c[2] ? {4'b0000, V2[15:4]} : V2;\n  wire        q3 = q2 | (qn_c[2] & (|V2[3:0]));\n  wire [15:0] V4 = qn_c[3] ? {8'b00000000, V3[15:8]} : V3;\n  wire        q4 = q3 | (qn_c[3] & (|V3[7:0]));\n  wire [10:0] W  = V4[10:0];\n  wire        s_sticky = q4 | sticky_low;\n  wire        s_round  = W[0] & (s_sticky | W[1]);\n  wire [9:0]  frac_s0  = W[10:1];\n  wire        csub     = s_round & &frac_s0[9:5] & &frac_s0[4:0];\n  wire [9:0]  sA       = andpre10(frac_s0);\n  wire [9:0]  frac_sr;\n  generate\n    assign frac_sr[0] = frac_s0[0] ^ s_round;\n    for (g = 1; g <= 8; g = g + 1)\n      assign frac_sr[g] = frac_s0[g] ^ (s_round & sA[g-1]);\n  endgenerate\n  assign frac_sr[9] = frac_s0[9] ^ csub;\n\n  // 8. late exponent assembly: E' = E0 + adj + c (overflow iff E' >= 31)\n  wire       infx  = inf_in;\n  wire [4:0] A0    = (ge31 | infx) ? 5'd31 : E0[4:0];\n  wire [4:0] A1    = (ge30 | infx) ? 5'd31 : E0p1[4:0];\n  wire [4:0] A2    = (ge29 | infx) ? 5'd31 : E0p2[4:0];\n  wire       N0    = (ge31 | infx);\n  wire       N1    = (ge30 | infx);\n  wire       N2    = (ge29 | infx);\n  wire [4:0] B1    = adj ? A2 : A1;\n  wire [4:0] B0    = adj ? A1 : A0;\n  wire       n1    = adj ? N2 : N1;\n  wire       n0    = adj ? N1 : N0;\n  wire [4:0] X5    = c  ? B1 : B0;\n  wire       n_inf = c  ? n1 : n0;\n  wire       sub_is = (adj ? le32 : le33) | zero_in;\n  wire       n_inf_frac = n_inf & ~sub_is;\n\n  // 9. pack\n  wire [4:0] exp_g  = sub_is ? {4'd0, csub} : X5;\n  wire [9:0] frac_g = sub_is ? frac_sr : frac_r;\n\n  wire [4:0] exp_y  = is_nan ? 5'd31 : exp_g;\n  wire [9:0] frac_y = is_nan ? 10'h200 : n_inf_frac ? 10'd0 : frac_g;\n\n  assign y = is_nan ? 16'h7E00 : {s_y, exp_y, frac_y};\n\n  // parallel-prefix AND (c[j] = &x[j:0], depth 4)\n  function [9:0] andpre10;\n    input [9:0] x;\n    integer j, l;\n    reg [9:0] c;\n    begin\n      c = x;\n      for (l = 0; l < 4; l = l + 1)\n        for (j = 9; j >= (1 << l); j = j - 1)\n          c[j] = c[j] & c[j - (1 << l)];\n      andpre10 = c;\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  // 12-bit Kogge-Stone prefix adder (sum fits in 12 bits)\n  function [11:0] pfx_add12;\n    input [11:0] xv, yv;\n    reg [11:0] g, p, gn, pn, px;\n    integer l, j, d;\n    begin\n      g  = xv & yv;\n      px = xv ^ yv;\n      p  = px;\n      for (l = 0; l < 4; l = l + 1) begin\n        d  = 1 << l;\n        gn = g;\n        pn = p;\n        for (j = 11; j >= d; j = j - 1) begin\n          gn[j] = g[j] | (p[j] & g[j-d]);\n          pn[j] = p[j] & p[j-d];\n        end\n        g = gn;\n        p = pn;\n      end\n      pfx_add12 = px ^ {g[10:0], 1'b0};\n    end\n  endfunction\nendmodule\n", filename='design_v16.v')

[ASSISTANT]
Found it — my "optimized" top-bit of the increment (`frac[g] = frac[g] ^ carry`) was wrong: bit 9 must use the standard increment (`^ (round & &lower_bits)`), since e.g. frac=511 + 1 = 512 needs bit 9 to become 1 while the carry-out goes to the exponent. Reverting those two lines:
  -> edit_file(filename='design_v16.v', new_string='  generate\n    assign frac_r[0] = frac_n[0] ^ n_round;\n    for (g = 1; g <= 9; g = g + 1)\n      assign frac_r[g] = frac_n[g] ^ (n_round & fA[g-1]);\n  endgenerate', old_string='  generate\n    assign frac_r[0] = frac_n[0] ^ n_round;\n    for (g = 1; g <= 8; g = g + 1)\n      assign frac_r[g] = frac_n[g] ^ (n_round & fA[g-1]);\n  endgenerate\n  assign frac_r[9] = frac_n[9] ^ c;')

## Summary

**Best result: `design_v15c.v` — PASS, cost 121,690** (area ≈ 90.4, delay ≈ 1347 @ target_delay 1300), vs. 145,429 for the starting point (~16% better).

### 1. Approaches tried
- **v1** (starting point copy): 22-bit LZC + bidirectional barrel shift + dual normal/subnormal paths → 145,429.
- **v3**: per-operand normalization (see below) → 138,947.
- **v8/v9**: one-hot leading-one decode + barrel shifter, prefix-AND rounding increments → 135,571 (v9's prefix-OR leading-one front end gave no gain).
- **v10**: fused normalizer (AND-OR left-justify instead of barrel shift) → 137,819, a regression; reverted.
- **v14**: truncated multiplier + adj-free subnormal shift → 132,040.
- **v15c**: early adj-free exponent bookkeeping + late 2-select mux → 127,340; then **target_delay tuning 1500→1400→1300** → **121,690**.
- **v16** (CSA + Kogge-Stone CPA, zero/inf folded into early exponent): fixes were applied but never verified — likely worth evaluating as-is next.

### 2. Highest-impact optimizations
1. **Per-operand normalization**: left-justify each significand's leading one to bit 10 (subnormals get exponent compensation), so the product always lies in [2²⁰, 2²²) — eliminates the 22-bit LZC and bidirectional shifter entirely; one unified exponent replaces two paths.
2. **Truncated multiplier**: only `prod_hi = prod[21:10] = 1024 + (ta+tb) + TT[19:10]` plus `sticky_low = |TT[9:0]|` are computed.
3. **Adj-free subnormal shift**: window anchored at `prod[10]` with `qn = 33−(EA+EB)` computed early; clamp shift ≥12→15 so "rounds to zero" needs no extra mux.
4. **Early exponent bookkeeping**: precompute `E0, E0+1, E0+2` and threshold flags; the late path is only a 2-select mux by `adj` and the rounding carry.
5. **target_delay sweep**: 1500→1300 alone cut ~4.5% with negligible area growth.

### 3. What caused regressions/failures
- Wrong shift direction/width in early normalizer versions (barrel shifter must be 11 bits — the subnormal's leading one IS its leading one, no hidden bit); wrong stage amounts (shift-3 instead of shift-8).
- A `wire [9:0] y` name shadowing the output port → lint/synthesis failure.
- Clamp condition `qn ≥ 12` mis-encoded (missed 16–19).
- The subtle one: `le32/le33` (S ≤ 33 tests) didn't mask `S[6]` — S reaches 126, so large exponents were misclassified subnormal, producing spurious zeros on overflow cases.
- v16's "optimized" top-bit increment (`frac[9] ^ carry`) is wrong; standard prefix increment (`frac[9] ^ (r & &frac[8:0])`) is required. Also: functions can't have non-constant loop bounds.

### 4. Lessons learned
- Write the value/exponent algebra derivation once, carefully, before coding — most failures were index/clamp mistakes, not architectural.
- Range-check every comparison: widths of intermediate sums vary (S is 7 bits, not 6).
- `edit_file` changes occasionally weren't reflected in evaluation; creating a fresh filename is the reliable path (also gives clean version history).
- Structural wins (truncation, moving logic earlier) beat micro-optimizations (prefix-OR LZC, fused normalizer); a short target_delay sweep after the RTL is stable is cheap and effective.
- Budget for debug: ~10 of 60 steps went to correctness bugs; keep each new variant a small delta over the last passing design.
